在努尔的地窖同阿布·哈利勒的酒馆之间，赛义德的夏日过得懒洋洋。一年的末一场雨落过了。每个退去的夜给城上留一顶擦亮的蓝华盖，太阳像一只火蜗，在上面慢慢地爬。卖水人的吆喝在众人耳中一日甜似一日；街狗睡着也吐着舌头喘。
每到傍晚，大批的人涌向沿岸园子里星星点点的游乐观所。人们坐在小凳上，或盘腿坐在地上，啜着杏仁、罗望子或玫瑰的香露，在溽暑一天的间歇里同鸟雀闲话；天烧成紫晶，又烧成翡翠，又烧成绿柱，树间大地的花色褪成一派百合的苍白。
有一回日落时分，赛义德去了拉希德的咖啡馆——他同塞利姆睡过一晚的地方——想打听打听旧伙计。可掌柜言语生硬、答得迟缓，赛义德便不再多问，从此也没再去。
每天清晨，天蒙蒙亮，老叫花子起身。就着努尔备下的酸奶和饼开了斋，他拄起手杖，奔哪座清真寺或哪道拱门去——那儿得又荫凉又有人流，是叫花子座位的两大要件。赛义德晚一个来钟头起，替女主人打了水、帮她拾擞了住处之后，一整天的闲工夫便都摊在手上。他在这个狞恶老太婆的宠爱里地位很高：一半无疑因为他随时肯干那些零碎小事，但主要还是因为他对她摆出的那副情郎做派。他敬她又恋她，拿她当灵魂的渴慕。
一个自由汉子伺候一个声名狼藉的老妇，这本身就滑稽；赛义德一旦哧摸出这层关系的荒诞味，便入戏十足，拿出信士的全部热忱来。奉承话和情热话哄得多了，他自己也险些忘了她那脂粉面具底下是一张老巫婆的脸；她呢，明知是哄，也把他疼成了眼珠子。
有时候，水烟壶的香雾叫他做了半个钟头的哲人，他便把这个老妇的命运同哈斯娜、同穷汉们的老婆比。

===Original===

Here was one whose name had been a byword for infamy living as a queen in her old age, extending bounty and protection to whom she would, exacting service as her due. The greatest of the city came under cover of the night to beseech her aid in secret business of the heart. Grand ladies of some notable’s harìm, veiled from all peril of recognition, sought her in their way from the bath or the perfumer’s on a like errand. Clandestine lovers made their heaven in her upper room. Each and all, fearing, blessed her and left gold in her hand. “Allah grant me as prosperous an old age!” thought Saïd. And yet Hasneh, the rough-handed and meanly clad, would have deemed herself the better of such an one. It was a strange thing!
Another person who had conceived a warm liking for the fisherman was the fat taverner. As the bright pattern of filial devotion, Saïd was always welcome to meat and drink and a narghileh afterwards in return for occasional help in the service of the coffeehouse. Abu Khalìl loved to ply him with parables and hard sayings, beginning always, “There was once a son,” and ending mostly in an attempt to cuff poor Camr-ud-dìn, the “son” in question. This unfortunate youth inherited his father’s tendency to fall asleep at odd moments. He would have become fat, too, like his father, had he been allowed to remain long enough in one spot. It was his constant chagrin that he could enjoy no rest, between waiting on customers and obeying his sire’s behests; for Abu Khalìl, though always dormant himself, would not let his son indulge in a moment’s lethargy. Camr-ud-dìn carried his grievance plainly written on his dirty brown face. He did everything under protest; and he loathed the sight of Saïd, who was forever being held up to him for an example.
Once or twice Saïd caught a glimpse of Selìm among the crowd in the streets, but on each occasion was able to dodge aside and avoid him. He would have rejoiced to know him happy and doing well, but was ashamed to meet him face to face. For this reason he shunned the great bazaars and more crowded ways in his walks abroad.
At least once in every day he was drawn to the house of Yuhanna the Christian. Sometimes he went thither at evening, when a deep earth shadow wrapped the city, and the western hill was black against an orange glow; more often in the early morning, while the ways were yet shady.

===Chinese===

这么一个名字当了淫贱代称的人，老了却活得像个王后，恩威自专，把伺候当应得。城里头一等的人物，趁夜色来求她办心上的秘密事。哪位大人物闺眷里的贵妇，遮得严严实实、绝无被人认出的风险，也打着浴场或香铺出来的路上，为一类的事来寻她。私会的情人们拿她的阁楼当天堂。人人怕她，个个为她祝福，把金子留在她掌心。「安拉也赐我一个这样兴旺的晚年吧！」赛义德想。可哈斯娜——那双粗手、那身寒衣的哈斯娜——却会自认比这样的人强。怪事！
对渔夫怀着一片热爱的还有一个，就是那胖掌柜。赛义德是孝行的光辉典范，咖啡馆里偶尔帮把手，就总有的吃、有的喝，饭后还有一壶水烟。阿布·哈利勒爱拿譬喻和难听话敲打他，起头永远是「从前有个儿子」，收尾多半是朝那位「儿子」——可怜的卡姆鲁丁——抡巴掌。这个倒霉的青年承了他爹的遗传，随时随地睡得着。他若被容许在一个地方待得够久，也会像他爹一样胖起来。他心头经年的委屈是不得安生：既要侍候客人，又要听他爹的差遣；阿布·哈利勒自己虽然长年打盹，却不容儿子懈怠一刻。卡姆鲁丁把这份冤屈明明白白写在他那张腕脏的棕脸上。他做什么都带着抗议；他恨见赛义德——那人永远被举出来给他做榜样。
有一两回，赛义德在街上的人群里瞥见了塞利姆，都及时闪身避开了。若知道他过得好、干得顺，他也会欢喜；只是没脸同他见面。因此出门时他绕开大巴扎，绕开人稠的路。
每一天，他至少要被基督徒尤汉纳的宅子牵去一回。有时是傍晚去——那时一片深深的地影裹着城，西山的黑影衬着橙色的余光；更多是清早，趁路上还有荫凉。

===Original===

Hid in the angle of the porch he could observe all that passed in the court within. The very stones of the pavement had charm for him. His beloved came and went, appeared and disappeared, now crooning a love-song with her baby brother in her arms, now mocking the coo of the pigeons, now romping with a maidservant. Whether she stood on tiptoe with head thrown back and arms uplifted, her long tresses reaching almost to her heels, to pull down the branch of a lemon-tree and see if a certain fruit were yellowing; whether she stamped her foot in sudden anger at the clumsiness of a servant, or slapped the child, who loved to bury his tiny hands in her hair and sometimes caused her pain﻿—whatever she did was full of grace in Saïd’s eyes. He would con over her moods and postures afterwards as he lay awake at night, tossing feverishly with a fire at his heart. Crouching in the shadow of the entrance he feasted his eyes on her beauty of form and motion, until someone came to disturb him, when he stole back in the blue shadow of narrow alleys, shunning instinctively the sunlight and open places, with a singing in his ears.
At such times he went not to the tavern of Abu Khalìl, but straight to the cellar of Nûr. The old woman listened kindly to his ravings, and soothed him with hints of hope, bidding him have but a little patience and he should be satisfied. The girl’s father, she said, was a wealthy merchant, a Nazarene, and under protection. It would be unsafe to carry her off in a time of quiet, for the Frankish consuls would be sure to clamour for vengeance. Alas, in these days none but a true believer could be wronged with impunity. But a change was at hand. Wherever she went﻿—in the palaces of the great as in the cellars of the poor﻿—she heard murmurs of discontent. Men’s forbearance was taxed to the utmost. A little more﻿—a feather, it must give way, and then Allah knew what would happen! There would be riot﻿—that at least was certain﻿—and amid the confusion of a whole city’s rising one girl could be abducted and no man know it. Saïd must therefore wait and trust in Allah.
He drew some momentary comfort from this assurance, but his desire grew with every day, threatening to consume him. Old Mustafa rejoiced secretly at the haggard looks of his young ally. He strove by all means to foster a longing which promised to fall in timely with his scheme of revenge.

===Chinese===

藏在门廊的拐角里，院里的一切动静尽收眼底。连铺路的石板于他都有魅力。他的心上人来来去去、忽隐忽现：一会儿抱着小弟弟哼情歌，一会儿学鸽子咕咕，一会儿同使女追打。她踮起脚，头后仰，胳膊高举，两条长辫几乎垂到脚跟，去扳柠檬树枝，看某一只果子黄了没有；她忽然跺脚，气哪个仆人的笨手笨脚；或是一巴掌打那孩子——他爱把小手埋进她的头发里，有时弄疼了她——无论她做什么，在赛义德眼里都是风姿万端。
夜里睡不着时，他便在心里一遍遍温习她的神情体态，心口烧着一团火，翻腾不已。他蹲在门口的荫影里，饱餐她身姿与动态的美，直到有人来搅扰，才溜回去——专捾窄巷的蓝影，本能地躲着日光和空场——耳朵里一片嗡鸣。
这样的时候他不去阿布·哈利勒的酒馆，径直回努尔的地窖。老太婆和颜悦色地听他胡话，拿希望的口风宽慰他，叫他只消稍安勿躁，必有满足之日。那姑娘的父亲，她说，是个殷实的商人，拿撒勒人，有官家庇护着。太平年月抢人不稳当，弗兰克人的领事们必定嚷着要报仇。唉，如今这世道，亏得只有真信士才吃得下。可变局就在眼前。她走到哪儿——大人物的宫里也好，穷人的地窖里也好——都听见怨声。人的忍耐已抽到了极限。再加一根——一根羽毛，它就得断；断了之后安拉才知道出什么事！要乱——这一条是准的——满城起事的乱局里，抢走一个姑娘神不知鬼不觉。所以赛义德必须等，必须托靠安拉。
这番担保给了他一时的安慰，可他的欲念一日盛似一日，眼看要烧掉他。老穆斯塔法暗暗欢喜他这位年轻同党的憔悴形容。他千方百计地浇灌这份饥渴——它眼看会同他的复仇大计正相衔接。

===Original===

He spoke rapturously of the charms of Yuhanna’s daughter when they sat together among the gardens in the pale evening; and he would hug himself with glee when the fisherman leapt up and cursed the day he was born, beseeching Allah to strike him dead, for what was life to him without his darling!
One morning, as Saïd lounged in the tavern of Abu Khalìl, a dehlibash entered, followed by an obsequious private. His uniform was that of the irregular troops distributed for a safeguard among the country towns and villages. He cast a keen glance round the coffeehouse, passing over Camr-ud-dìn and his father and two Christian lads drinking arak together in a corner, until his eye rested on Saïd.
“Yonder is the man for us﻿—what sayest thou, ’brahìm?”
“A strong man!﻿—a fine man!” agreed the soldier, bending his right arm and feeling the muscle thereof to confirm his meaning.
“Look here, O what is thy name?” said the officer, addressing Saïd; “if thou hast a mind to earn ten piastres, rise up and follow me!”
If he wished to earn ten piastres! O day of blessing! O day of good luck! Upon his head he would serve his Excellency. To hear was to obey. Might Allah preserve his Honour’s life forever! What might be his Grace’s further orders?
The officer strode out of the tavern again, motioning him to walk with the private soldier. In this order they traversed the city. Passing out at an eastern gate they came to a wide-open space where grass grew in ragged patches. Under some big trees which bordered the parade ground was a motley gathering of men and horses. The arrival of the dehlibash was hailed with loud blessing and cringing salaams. A steed was apportioned to Saïd, while the officer counted his men.
“Praise to Allah, the tale is complete!” he said with a sigh of relief; and then, looking at his watch, “It is lucky that it is so, for it wants but a half of the appointed hour. Here, ’brahìm, let this man wear thy paletot and give him a gun! At present he has nothing of the soldier about him. At an ordinary time it does not matter; but a friend whispered me this morning that the Wâly himself purposes to review us; and it is likely Abdul Cader will be with him. He is a great general by Allah, is Abdul Cader﻿—his eyes are as the eyes of an eagle.

===Chinese===

苍茫的傍晚，两人坐在园子里，他就如痴如醉地夸尤汉纳女儿的好处；渔夫跳起来咒自己生辰、求安拉劈死他——没有心上人，命算什么！——这时他便搂着自己窃喜。
一天早上，赛义德正在阿布·哈利勒的酒馆里闲坐，进来一个台里巴什，身后跟着个胁肩谄笑的大兵。他穿的制服属于那种散在各乡镇「保境」的非正规军。他把咖啡馆锐利地扫了一圈——掠过卡姆鲁丁父子，掠过角落里同饮茴香酒的两个基督徒小子——眼睛最后落在赛义德身上。
「那边那个正合咱们的用——你说呢，易卜拉欣？」
「一条壮汉！——好样的！」那兵附和，弯起右臂，捏了捏臂上的肌肉，坐实他的意思。
「喂，你叫什么来着？」军官朝赛义德说，「你要有心挣十个皮阿斯特，就起来跟我走！」
他要有心挣十个皮阿斯特！有福的日子！走红运的日子！赴汤蹈火也愿为大人效劳。听命即遵行。愿安拉保大人千岁！大人还有什么吩咐？
军官大步出了酒馆，示意他同那大兵同行。就这个次序，三人穿城而过。出了东门，来到一片开阔地，草东一丛西一簇地长着。操场边上几棵大树底下，聚着一群杂七杂八的人和马。台里巴什一到，祝祷声、躬身礼响成一片。给赛义德分了一匹马，军官清点他的人。
「赞美安拉，一个不缺！」他松了口气说；随即看了看表，「幸好一个不缺，离定下的时辰只差半个钟头了。喂，易卜拉欣，把你的外套给他披上，再给他一条枪！他现在浑身上下没有一点兵样。平常时候不打紧；可有朋友今早咬耳朵告诉我，瓦利大人要亲自来检阅；阿卜杜勒·卡迪尔多半也随行。凭安拉说，阿卜杜勒·卡迪尔是位大将军——他那双眼，是鹰的眼。

===Original===

Well”﻿—he shrugged his shoulders and spread out his hands in deprecation﻿—“if the Government can only afford to pay seventy soldiers and I am obliged to maintain a hundred, is it my fault that they be not clothed like the Sultàn’s bodyguard?”
Saïd donned the soldier’s overcoat. The hood hanging between his shoulders irked him like a burden, so that he twisted his neck to see what was there, provoking shouts of laughter. Then he swung the carbine across his back, just as the order to mount was given.
The dehlibash marshalled his troop, two deep, in the middle of the parade ground. Even thus, in the full glare of the sun, with glint of gun-barrels and prancing of steeds, the show was not a brave one. A few half-naked urchins, smitten with awe at the sight, stood to watch, and idlers from the city gathered to the spot. Presently there was a noise of shouting and a pair of outriders cantered out at the gate, followed at an interval of about a hundred yards by a group of horsemen in civil dress surrounding the person of the Wâly.
Ahmed Pasha wore the official frock coat and dark trousers, his sober Frankish garb contrasting strangely with the gorgeous trappings of his charger. His pale, intellectual face appeared the whiter for the scarlet fez pressed low on the forehead. Beside him, on the left hand, rode that great one whom Saïd had seen in the court of the mosque, sitting with the Mufti and Ismaìl Abbâs. Two Franks, whose top-boots were very prominent, rode on the Wâly’s right, and a servile official or two completed the party.
“Who is he?” Saïd inquired of a neighbour in the ranks.
“Who? O stupid! Ahmed Basha, of course!”
“No, I speak not of the Wâly; but say, who is that great one who rides at his left hand?”
“Whence comest thou? Who art thou who dwellest in Es-shâm and dost not know Abdul Cader, the mighty chief of Eljizar whom the French took and imprisoned and at length banished hither! Hist!”
The troop saluted after a fashion, and the Wâly began his ride along the ranks, chiefly to ascertain that the right number of men were there. He seemed mortified by the wretched appearance of the troop. The two Franks smiled openly, pointing out individual scarecrows one to the other. As luck would have it, something in Saïd’s bearing pleased Ahmed Pasha.

===Chinese===

再说」——他耸耸肩，摊开双手以示无奈——「官府只养得起七十个兵，我却不得不凑一百个；他们穿得不像苏丹的御林军，难道是我的错？」
赛义德披上兵的外套。垂在两肩之间的风帽硌得他像背了件重物，他扭着脖子想看背后是什么，惹起一片哄笑。他把马枪往背上一甩，上马的口令恰好下了。
台里巴什把队伍在操场当中排成两列。就这样，顶着大太阳，枪管闪着光，战马踏着步，这场面也实在算不上雄壮。几个半裸的小顽童看得肃然起敬，站着不走；城里的闲人也朝这儿聚。不一会儿一片喝道声，两名前导骑出了城门，小跑着来；隔约一百步，是一群便装的骑手，簇拥着瓦利大人。
艾哈迈德帕夏穿着官家的黑礼服和深色裤子，那身素净的弗兰克打扮，同他坐骑的华美鞍辔对照得古怪。他苍白文秀的脸，因低扣在额前的猩红菲斯帽，显得愈发白。他左手边并辔骑着的，正是赛义德在清真寺院子里见过的那位大人物——同穆夫提和伊斯玛仪·阿拔斯坐在一处的。两名弗兰克人骑在瓦利右手，长统马靴扎眼得很；再加一两个胁肩的属官，一行人就齐了。
「那是谁？」赛义德问队伍里挨着他的人。
「谁？蠢材！当然是艾哈迈德帕夏！」
「不，我说的不是瓦利；我问，他左手边骑马的那位大人物是谁？」
「你打哪儿来？住在沙姆的人，竟不认得阿卜杜勒·卡迪尔——阿尔及尔的豪杰首领，法国人拿住他、关了他、末了把他放逐到这儿来的！嘘！」
队伍马马虎虎行了个礼，瓦利开始沿队列骑行——主要还是点验人数对不对。队伍这副寒碜相像是叫他脸上挂不住。两个弗兰克人公然含笑，彼此指点着一个个稻草人。也是运气，赛义德的做派里有点什么，中了艾哈迈德帕夏的意。

===Original===

He reined in his horse before him and made a remark over his shoulder to the Franks, who drew near with expectant faces.
“Now, my man, thou that art a servant of justice in this province, I put a case to thee: Suppose thou foundest a Muslim and a Christian fighting together, what wouldst thou do?”
Saïd reflected a moment.
“May it please your Excellency, I should take the Kâfir to prison.”
The Wâly bit his lip and rode on. The Franks tried in vain to stifle their laughter. Even Abdul Cader smiled and his eyes twinkled.
His round of inspection over, Ahmed Pasha addressed some sharp words of admonition to the troops; and refusing to listen to the officer’s excuses, rode back again into the city. The crowd which had followed the governor dispersed after him. The soldiers retired to the shade of the plane-trees and there dismounted. Saïd and some fifty other faggots were paid off; and, being deprived of their guns and such soldierly garments as they had assumed for the nonce, sauntered away as civilians.
In his road to the tavern of Abu Khalìl, the fisherman saw signs of unwonted excitement. The faces of the men he met had a fierce and eager look. Once or twice a Nazarene passed him, slinking along by the wall with the furtive side-glance of a dog that one stones. Drivers of camels and mules who seemed to come from the way of the mountains were beset by an eager crowd begging for news; while others coming off the desert passed unheeded save for the curses of those whom the advance of their laden beasts threatened to crush against the wall. Khans and coffeehouses were full to overflowing, and the sound of many voices in agitation came from their shadowy doorways. It was near noon﻿—an hour when men are wont to move lazily, and the very camels seem to slumber as they rock heavily onward with jangling bells. But today all was animation. Even the street-dogs opened an eye, drew in their tongues at intervals and stirred uneasily in their sleep.
Saïd accosted two men who were arguing and gesticulating in the shade of a merchant’s awning.
“What is the news?” he asked.
“Great news, O my uncle﻿—news of moment! There is war in the Mountain and it is sure that the Mowarni have arisen and have destroyed twenty villages belonging to the Drûz. One that has but now arrived from Beyrût assured me of it.

===Chinese===

他在赛义德跟前勒住马，回头朝弗兰克人说了句什么，那两位带着期待的脸上前来。
「喂，汉子，你是本省公道的公仆，我考你一题：假设你撞见一个穆斯林同一个基督徒打架，你怎么办？」
赛义德想了一想。
「回大人，我把那卡菲尔送进牢里。」
瓦利咬了咬嘴唇，继续往前走。两个弗兰克人忍笑忍了个白费。连阿卜杜勒·卡迪尔也笑了，眼睛一亮。
巡阅一圈完事，艾哈迈德帕夏向队伍训了几句尖利的话；军官的辩解也不听，打马回城去了。跟着总督的人群随他散去。兵们退到悬铃木的荫里下马。赛义德同另外五十来个凑数的柴禾领了工钱，缴了枪、脱下那身临时披挂的兵衣，又成了老百姓，信步走散。
往阿布·哈利勒酒馆去的路上，渔夫看见一片异样的骚动。迎面男人的脸上都带着凶急之色。有一两回，拿撒勒人打他身边过，贴着墙根溜，侧眼偷觑，像一条挨惯了石头的狗。从山那边来的驼夫骡夫，教一群心急的人围住讨消息；从沙漠那头来的却无人理会——只有他们的重载把人往墙上挤时，才换得几句咒骂。客店和咖啡馆人满为患，幽暗的门口涌出一片鼎沸。时近正午——往常这个时辰人们懒洋洋地，连骆驼都像睡着，驮着重载、和着乱铃一摇一晃往前挪。今天却处处是活气。连街狗也睁开一只眼，时不时收一收舌头，睡梦里不安地动。
赛义德向两个正在一家商铺凉棚荫里争辩比划的人搭话。
「什么消息？」他问。
「大消息，老丈——了不得的消息！山里打起来了，千真万确是马龙派起了事，毁了德鲁兹人二十座村子。一位刚从贝鲁特到的，亲口向我保的。

===Original===

He saw the flames like stars on all the seaward slopes as he passed the ridge at sundown. It is sin, by Allah! for the Drûz are our brothers in this matter.”
“Nay, by Allah! it is a lying report thou hast heard!” cried the other man, vehemently. “It is the Drûz who have risen up suddenly and have destroyed thirty villages of the Mowarni. It is true, however, what thou sayest, that the Drûz are our brothers. May their power increase!”
The merchant before whose shop they were squabbling removed the ivory mouthpiece of a narghileh from his lips and crossed his legs more comfortably.
“It is likely both of you are wrong,” he said. “The event occurred only yesterday, so the tidings are not yet confirmed. This is but the first rumour which we hear. It is surely greater than the truth.”
Saïd hurried on his way with a full heart. Wild fancies, that were half hope and half project, throbbed in his mind. The time foretold of the lawyer was come; the day to which Mustafa looked for vengeance was at hand. A fire was kindled on Lebanon, and a strong wind blew from the sea. The smoke was driven over the great city, and there were sparks in the smoke. Es-Shâm was as a heap of tinder carefully prepared. Through vague pictures of riot and bloodshed he saw the daughter of Yuhanna as he had first seen her, fondling her baby brother in a blue shadow which the intervening sunlight dusted with gold. The vision was perfect even to the purple flowers on the wall at her back and a pair of white butterflies sporting above her head. The vividness of it pained Saïd, causing heart and brain to ache.
The tavern of Abu Khalìl was crowded and uproarious when he reached it. Just within the threshold, forced outward by the press, stood the host himself with back to the sunlight. By his manner of standing he seemed anxious and ill at ease. The expression of his face when he turned was the same which Saïd had seen it wear when knives were drawn in the house or a customer flew at another’s throat. With a touch of the hand and a whispered salutation the fisherman slipped past him and edged his shoulder into the throng. Stools overturned were being kicked about among the feet of the disputants. Clenched hands were shaken fiercely in angry faces. Every man believed himself to be possessed of the truth of the matter and resented his neighbour’s statement.
“Thirty villages!”﻿—“Twenty!”

===Chinese===

日落翻山时，他望见海那面的山坡上处处火光，像星星。凭安拉说，这是罪过！德鲁兹人在这一桩上是我们兄弟。」
「不，凭安拉说！你听的是谣言！」另一个激烈地嚷。「是德鲁兹人突然起了事，毁了马龙派三十座村子。不过你有一句说得对：德鲁兹人是我们兄弟。愿他们的力量壮大！」
两人吵嘴的那家铺子的掌柜，把水烟壶的象牙嘴从唇边拿下，把腿盘得更舒服了些。
「多半你们两个都错了，」他说。「事情是昨天才出的，信儿还没坐实。咱们听到的不过是头一拨谣言。谣言准比实情大。」
赛义德怀着满膛的心事赶路。狂乱的念头在脑子里突突地跳——一半是希望，一半是计划。律师预言的时辰到了；穆斯塔法盼着报仇的日子到门口了。黎巴嫩起了火，海上刮来大风。烟吹过大城，烟里有火星。沙姆像一堆细心备好的引柴。透过暴乱和流血的模糊图景，他看见尤汉纳的女儿，同初见时一模一样：在一方蓝荫里逗着小弟弟，中间隔着的日光洒着金粉。那景象纤毫毕现——连她背后墙上的紫花、头顶嬉戏的一对白蝶都在。这份逼真叫赛义德发疼，心疼，脑子也疼。
他赶到阿布·哈利勒的酒馆时，馆里挤翻了天。门槛里侧，叫人潮挤得半个身子在外，站着掌柜本人，背朝日光。那站相透着焦灼不安。他转过脸来的神情，同赛义德见过的两回一模一样——一回是馆里亮了刀子，一回是客人掐上了对方的喉咙。渔夫手一碰、口里低道一声安好，从他身边溜过，用肩膀楔进人堆。推翻的小凳在争辩者的脚底下踢来踢去。攋紧的拳头在一张张怒脸前狠狠挥。人人都自信独得了这件事的真相，邻座一开口就遭他抢白。
「三十座村子！」——「二十座！」

===Original===

﻿—“No, a hundred, I tell thee!”﻿—“The Drûz, by the Quran!”﻿—“The Christians for certain!”
In the thickest of the crush Saïd descried an emerald mantle edged with fur. It shone out brightly amid the ruck of soiled robes of every conceivable colour, blue predominating. An embroidered turban binding a newish fez was conspicuous in like manner. The young lawyer, who came thither to converse with clients, was struggling to obtain a hearing.
“I who am a lawyer tell you that it behoves all men to keep peace at this crisis!” Saïd heard him cry. “Let the unbelievers extirpate each other﻿—Durzi and Marûni. The Franks are powerful and wish ill to Islâm. They will cause all who take part against the Christians to be put to death. What profit has a man though he destroy his enemies if he die for it? The Wâly has summoned the Council of Notables. They will take strong measures to prevent a disturbance. Calm your minds, I entreat you, all of you!”
Derisive shouts drowned his prayers. The old beggar sprang forward and gripped his shoulder. He swung the lawyer round so that he could grin in his face.
“What is this, effendi?” he said with a mad laugh. “Does a man change his mind with each moon? A little while since, when the chance of war seemed remote, thou wast a lion, exhorting us to battle with brave words. But now, on the eve of the tumult thy heart grows faint. In the beginning, when there is but a spark, it is easy to fan it or blow it out, whichever one please; but afterward, when it is become a great fire all the breath of a man avails not to extinguish it. Courage, O Excellency! It is a creditable thing to be chief among men. Be sure I will give thee all honour, and praise thee as my leader in this business!”
With an oath the lawyer tore himself away. His face was vivid as he pushed through the noisy crowd to the door. He passed quite close to Saïd, so that the latter could hear him mutter under his breath﻿—
“A madman﻿—dangerous to the peace of the city﻿—I go straight to denounce him. With Allah’s leave he shall be in a gaol ere sunset!”
Saïd watched him shuffle away in the direction of the Wâly’s house, keeping close to the castle wall, as though its strength were a protection, the skirts of his emerald coat bellying behind him.

===Chinese===

——「不，一百座，我告诉你！」——「德鲁兹人干的，凭古兰经！」——「准是基督徒！」
人堆最密处，赛义德瞥见一领镶毛边的翡翠斗篷。它在各色各样、蓝的居多的脏袍子中间，亮得扎眼。一条绣花头巾缠着顶半新菲斯帽，同样惹眼。那位来会当事人的年轻律师，正拼命要人听他说话。
「我是个律师，我告诉你们，这危难关头，人人以安静为要！」赛义德听见他喊。「由着那些不信教的互相杀绝——德鲁兹也好，马龙派也好。弗兰克人势大，存心害伊斯兰。谁动手打基督徒，他们就叫谁偿命。毁了仇家、自己却搭上性命，于人有什么益处？瓦利已传了缙绅议事会。他们会下狠手防乱。安下心来吧，我求你们，求你们众人！」
嘲弄的吼声淹了他的祈求。老叫花子蹿上前，攋住他的肩，把律师拧过身来，好冲着他的脸龇牙。
「这是怎么说的，埃芬迪？」他狂笑着说。「人心随月亮一月一换么？前些日子战事还远，你是头狮子，拿豪言壮语劝我们上阵。如今乱子在即，你的心倒怯了。起头只有一粒火星时，要煽要吹灭，随人的便，都容易；到后来烧成大火，一个人的气力吹也吹不灭了。拿出胆来，大人！为人上人，是件体面事。你放心，我必敬你，颂你做我这桩事的首领！」
律师咒骂一声，挣脱了。他挤过喧闹的人群向门口去，脸色鲜明。他打赛义德身边擦过，近得赛义德听得见他压着嗓子的咕哝——
「一个疯子——危害全城治安——我这就去告他。托安拉的福，日落之前叫他进牢！」
赛义德望着他蹭着走远，朝瓦利府的方向去，紧贴着城堡的墙根，仿佛那墙的坚固能护住他，翡翠外衣的后襟在背后鼓着风。

===Original===

Then he elbowed his way to where Mustafa was leaping and dancing like a maniac in the midst of the press, screaming curses on the Christians to the joy of all.
Saïd plucked his robe and whispered, but the old man shook him off at first and raved more frantically than ever. But by dint of repeating his warning in a louder tone, and dragging him by main force towards the door, he at length won him to hear reason. They went out together into the blinding sunshine, Mustafa cursing all lawyers and their kinsfolk.
On reaching the cellar where they lodged, “Allah is gracious! The time is come, O Nûr!” cried Mustafa, capering and waving his skinny arms in a frenzy of glee.

===Chinese===

随后他挤到穆斯塔法那里——老头正在人堆当中蹦跳如狂，尖声咒骂基督徒，骂得众人快活。
赛义德扯他的袍子，附耳低语；老头起先甩开他，闹得更凶。赛义德把警告一遍遍提高嗓门，又连拉带拽把他往门口拖，末了终于说动他听了道理。两人一同走进晃眼的阳光里，穆斯塔法一路咒着天下所有的律师及其亲族。
到了落脚的地窖，「安拉仁慈！时辰到了，努尔！」穆斯塔法喊，欢腾得直蹦，干瘦的胳膊乱挥。

### XXI / 二十一
===Original===

In those days the taverns of the city were never empty except at dead of night. Each sun brought fresh tidings of a rousing nature; and the excitement of the vulgar is a gossip who must chatter or die. It was soon known for certain that the Maronites had been the aggressors in the first place; but now the Drûz were slaying them like sheep all along the mountain.
“Of a surety, the Drûz are our brothers!” was the judgment of every true believer. “It is not true, what is commonly told of them, that they worship a calf in secret places. By the Quran they are no idolaters. They fall not prostrate before pictures of women and sheep, as do the Nazarenes; but worship Allah even as we do. May they utterly destroy their enemies, who are ours also!”
Men went about their work distractedly with brains on fire. Unrest was everywhere. The sunlight itself, which baked the roofs, quivered of anticipation. The crescent gleaming on dome and minaret had a message for all the faithful.
Only in the Christian quarter fear reigned amid a deathlike hush. The few inhabitants who ventured beyond its limits were hustled and spit upon. True believers cursed and reviled them so that they grovelled in terror of their lives. There was menace in the very air, so that they breathed it with deprecation.
In the dewy shade of an early morning Saïd bent his steps towards the house of Yuhanna. Wrapt in thought of his beloved he walked as in a dream. The ways were cool, he was conscious of a strip of radiance overhead, he saw men move as shadows. At a joyful shout of his own name he started as though one had struck him.
“Is it indeed thou, O Selìm?” he cried. “O day of joy! How goes thy business?”
The memory of his former scurvy treatment of the muleteer made him a little backward in cordiality. But upon Selìm embracing him tenderly as a brother, with no more than a playful reproach on the score of his desertion, he was truly delighted to see him once again; and they walked on, hand in hand, so far as their roads lay together. Saïd had little to relate. His life since their parting had been lazy and uneventful. Of the all-absorbing topic of Yuhanna’s daughter he cared not to speak, being far from secure of his friend’s approval. But Selìm, on the other hand, had much to tell.

===Chinese===

那些日子里，这城的酒馆除了深更半夜从不清空。每一个日头都带来新鲜撩人的消息；而市井的兴奋是个碎嘴子，不嚼舌头就得死。很快就确切知道了：头一拳是马龙派先动的；可如今德鲁兹人正沿山一路把他们像宰羊一样宰。
「千真万确，德鲁兹人是俺们的兄弟！」每个真信士都这么断。「外头说他们暗地里拜牛犊，那不是真的。凭古兰经说，他们不是拜偶像的。他们不朝女人的像、羊的像下拜——拿撒勒教徒才那样；他们拜安拉，跟俺们一个拜法。叫他们把他们的仇敌——也就是俺们的仇敌——斩尽杀绝！」
人们脑子烧着火，魂不守舍地做着各自的活计。处处是不安。连烤着屋顶的日光本身，都颤着一份预感。穹顶和宣礼塔上闪着的新月，给全体信士捎着一封口信。
只有基督徒街区，一片死寂里盘踞着恐惧。敢迈出街区的那寥寥几个居民，叫人推搡、啐唾沫。真信士们咒他们骂他们，骂得他们趴在地上哆嗦，只求保命。连空气里都带着威胁，他们连呼吸都赔着小心。
一个清晨，趁露水未干的荫凉，萨义德把脚步迈向尤汉纳的宅子。他想着他的心肝，一路像在梦里。道上凉凉的，他觉出头顶一线亮，看见人影子似的动。忽然一声欢呼喊他的名字，他一惊，像叫人打了一记。
「真是你吗，塞利姆？」他喊，「快乐的日子！你的差事怎么样？」
想起先前那样亏待这骡夫，他热络得有点滞涩。可塞利姆兄弟似的亲亲热热抱住他，对他那场开溜不过玩笑似的埋怨了一句，他这才真心欢喜重见他；两人手拉手走了一程，走到两条路岔开为止。萨义德没什么可讲的：分手以后，他的日子过得又懒又没动静。至于那个占满他心思的题目——尤汉纳的女儿——他不想提，他对这位朋友的赞成远没有把握。倒是塞利姆，有一车话要讲。

===Original===

Alone, he had carried on the old business for a few days, in the hope of Saïd’s return; but things had not thriven with him. The voice of the master was gone, and he might shout till he was hoarse in praise of the wares, yet few paused to examine them. So he sold the remnant of his stock to a dealer for what it would fetch, and journeyed to the mountain-village where was his home, to dandle his baby and take counsel with his woman. On his return to the city he applied for help to Ismaìl Abbâs, the Sherìf﻿—Saïd remembered?﻿—who received him very kindly and gave him a letter﻿—guess to whom! to Ahmed Pasha, to his Highness the Wâly himself! In short, he was now a member of the Governor’s household, receiving bakshìsh from all desirous to curry favour in his master’s neighbourhood.
He was in the way of honour, and (under Allah) he thanked Saïd for it. Had it not been for that rich garment Saïd gave him he would never have caught the eye of the great Ismaìl Abbâs in the first instance. Moreover, he praised his friend’s generosity and self-denial in that he had not taken his share of the slender profits of their partnership away with him. It was a magnanimous action, but then Saïd was ever the father of kindness. He had grieved much for the loss of his brother, and had even been to the cellar of Nûr seeking news of him. But the mistress of the house﻿—a tall old woman with painted eyes﻿—had been short with him and he could learn nothing from her.
Saïd’s heart smote him as he listened. Allah had blessed him with the truest friend ever man had, and he had slighted the gift. He squeezed Selìm’s hand and swung it lightly to and fro as they walked. Might Allah destroy him utterly and quench the fire on his hearth if ever again he gave this good man cause to reproach him.
“I rejoice in thy happiness,” he said when the time came for them to part. “And what is the mind of his Excellency the Basha with respect to the war of the Mountain? Wait a little and there shall be war in Es-Shâm on the pattern of it.”
“Alas, O Saïd, they say in the palace of my lord that should the men of Es-Shâm follow the example of the Drûz, then the downfall of Islâm is sure, for the Franks will avenge the Nazarenes, that is known. The Wâly himself is very anxious: it is said that he weeps at night in his chamber. He is a great general of renown, but he loves study better than government.

===Chinese===

独个儿，他把老营生撑了几天，巴望萨义德回来；可他手气不顺。主子的嗓音没了：他把货夸到嗓子哑，也没几个人停脚。他把剩货照市价倒给一个贩子，回他山里的家，抱了抱娃，跟他女人合计了一回。回城之后，他投奔了谢里夫·伊斯梅尔·阿巴斯——萨义德记得吧？——对方待他极客气，给了他一封信——你猜给谁！给艾哈迈德帕夏，给瓦利殿下本人！一句话，他如今是总督府里的人了，凡想在老爷近旁讨好的，都得给他巴克什希。
他走的是体面的道，这一是（凭安拉说）托萨义德的福。不是萨义德赏他那件阔袍，伟大的伊斯梅尔·阿巴斯头一眼也看不上他。再说，他夸他朋友慷慨克己：合伙那点薄利，萨义德分文没带走。那是大丈夫的行止，不过萨义德本来就是善心之父。他为兄弟的失踪难过了好久，还上努尔的地窖打听过消息。可那家主妇——一个画眼的高个子老太婆——三言两语把他打发了，他一个字也没套出来。
萨义德听着，心里挨了一鞭。安拉赐他天下顶真的朋友，他却慢待了这份赏。他攦紧塞利姆的手，一路轻轻甩着走。他若再叫这个好人有一句可埋怨的，叫安拉灭了他、灭了他灶上的火。
「你的造化，俺欢喜。」分手的时辰到了，他说。「帕夏大人对山里的战事是个什么章程？等一等瞧，沙姆这就要照着那个样子也来一场。」
「唉，萨义德，俺主子宫里的人说，大马士革人要是学了德鲁兹人的样，伊斯兰就完了：法兰克人必替拿撒勒教徒报仇，这是明摆的。瓦利本人愁得很：据说他夜里在屋里哭。他是成名的大将军，可他爱读书胜过爱做官。

===Original===

One of the soldiers of the guard, who has served under him in the wars of Europe, tells me that he was ever a great general﻿—none greater﻿—upon paper: victory waited on his science; but he loved not the turmoil of a battle and its perils.
“His mind is now torn asunder by the demands of the Franks wishing one thing, and the advice of the elders of Islâm, who desire the opposite. In truth, it seems to me who am a small man and no politician, that he hearkens too willingly to the speeches of the Franks, the sworn enemies of the Faith. It was no wise thing that he did yesterday in ordering the dog Jurji, who did outrage on the harìm of Asad Effendi, to be released without punishment. The Franks speak as lawyers on behalf of their clients, and they strengthen their pleading by threats. This pardon of an evildoer, simply because he is a Nazarene, will madden the faithful. As I came just now through the long bazaar, a band of youths armed with sticks passed me, running towards the Christian quarter, vowing they would do justice on Jurji with their own hands. I fear the Wâly has been ill-advised in this matter. He is a great man and a politic, but he is weak, and the Franks overbear him. I fear there will be trouble. Thanks be to Allah that Selìm is not the great Wâly of Damashc-ush-Shâm, but only a small servant whose duty is plain. May Allah guard thee in safety till we meet again!”
They parted. Selìm was quickly lost in the shifting crowd of a roofed bazaar, while Saïd, striking into a quiet alley, pursued his way to the house of Yuhanna. The news of the release of Jurji rankled in his mind, making him venomous towards the Christians.
As he passed the threshold of the outer door, seeking that corner of the entrance passage whence he was used to spy on his delight, he stumbled on a pitcher someone had left there. The earthern vessel crashed upon the stones and was shattered to bits. The noise was enough to bring the whole household running to the spot. Bitterly cursing the accident, Saïd took to his heels. A little way up the lane he hid himself in the angle of two walls.
Presently, as he stood there waiting till the alarm of the broken pot should have had time to subside, he heard loud voices approaching.

===Chinese===

卫队里一个兵，跟他在欧洲打过仗，告诉俺：他是纸上顶大的将军——大得没人比——纸上处处得胜；可他不爱战场的乱和险。
「他这会儿叫两边撕扯着：法兰克人要一头，伊斯兰的长老们劝另一头。说实在的，俺一个小人物、不懂政治，瞧他太肯听法兰克人的话了——他们是信仰的死敌。他昨天办的事就不聪明：放了那条狗朱尔吉，不治罪——就是污了阿萨德·埃芬迪内闱的那个。法兰克人替他们的主顾说话，活像讼师，还拿威胁加固他们的状子。一个作恶的，单凭他是拿撒勒教徒就赦了，这要把信士们逼疯。俺方才穿过长巴扎，一伙拿棍子的少年从俺身边跑过去，直奔基督徒街区，赌咒要亲手给朱尔吉一个公道。俺怕瓦利这件事上失了算。他是大人物，有权谋，可他软，法兰克人压他。俺怕要出乱子。感谢安拉，塞利姆不是大马士革的大瓦利，只是个小仆人，本分看得清清楚楚。叫安拉保你平安，等俺们再见！」
两人分了手。塞利姆很快没进一带带顶巴扎的人流里，萨义德拐进一条僻静小巷，往尤汉纳的宅子去。朱尔吉获释的消息在他心里发炎，叫他朝基督徒这边冒毒。
跨进外门门槛，正要摸向门道里他惯常窥看他那份欢喜的那个角落，他绊上了谁摺在那里的一只水罐。陶罐砸在石头上，碎成了一把渣。这一声足够叫全家奔到出事地点。萨义德狠狠咒了一句，撒腿就跑。往巷子上头走了一小截，他闪进两面墙的夹角里藏了。
他正站在那儿，等打碎罐子的那阵惊动平息，忽然听见大声的人语逼近。

===Original===

A rabble of Muslim lads burst into the narrow way, cursing all the Nazarenes, and yelling that they were come to do justice on Jurji the evildoer and destroy his father’s house with fire. Most of them carried sticks; some had long knives in their hands. Seeing a man look out from the door of Yuhanna’s house they chalked the sign of the cross ostentatiously on the pavement, spat upon it, and trampled it underfoot. The head was quickly withdrawn and the door shut and bolted from within.
This seemed rare sport to Saïd. Lifting up his voice against the Christians, he joined himself to the mob.
They paraded the entire quarter, reviling all they met. Here and there a man cried shame upon them, but the most part slunk past them along the wall with a cringing salutation. At length, growing weary of their unchallenged progress, they were about to disperse, when a happy thought occurred to Saïd. He imparted it to his comrades, who were loud in acclamation. Such as had knives set to work to cut short lengths of stick, which they bound two and two together so as to form rough crosses. Then they took hold of the street-dogs, which lay around them by dozens, tied a cross under the tail of each, and with a kick sent them howling in all directions.
The fun was at its height when a man dressed in the Frankish fashion, but swarthy and wearing a fez, emerged from a doorway close by in earnest conversation with a Muslim in a fur-edged mantle of emerald green. He of the foreign garb cast one searching glance at the crowd, and then, seeing its occupation, walked off hurriedly, dragging the lawyer along with him.
“Dìn Muhammed!” Saïd yelled after them in derision. “Behold we follow thy advice, effendi!”
“Dìn Muhammed﻿—Allah! Allah! Perish the unbelievers!” shouted a few of his companions; but the greater part were silent, seeming afraid.
“It is the dragoman of the Muscovite Consul,” one murmured with consternation. “He knows me well, whose son I am. He will surely lodge information against us and we shall be imprisoned for this day’s work.”
“Let us after and slay him!” cried another, valorous from a whole morning spent in insulting men with impunity.
“Let us go quietly each to his own place!” pleaded a third, who had cause for alarm, being well-known to the dragoman.
His advice seemed best to all, and they disbanded forthwith.

===Chinese===

一伙穆斯林小子涌进窄巷，咒骂全体拿撒勒教徒，嚷着他们是来惩办恶徒朱尔吉、拿火烧他爹的宅子的。大多拿着棍子，有几个手里攦着长刀。看见一个人从尤汉纳家门口探头，他们拿粉笔在石板上惹眼地画了个十字，啐上去，又一脚踏烂。那颗脑袋飞快缩了回去，门关上，里头落了闩。
这在萨义德瞧来是好戏。他扬起嗓子骂基督徒，入了伙。
他们游遍整个街区，见谁骂谁。这里那里也有人喎一声不要脸，可大多数贴着墙根溜过去，赔一躬到地。末了，一路无人拦阻，他们逛腻了，正要散，萨义德冒出个好主意。他授意给同伙，同伙齐声叫好。带刀的动手把长棍截成短截，两根两根绑作一个粗陋的十字。然后他们拎起满街卧着的野狗——成十成打——每条尾巴底下拴一个十字，一脚踢出去，叫它们嚎着四散。
闹到兴头上，一个法兰克装束的男人——皮肤黝黑，头戴毡帽——从近旁一个门洞出来，正同一个穿翡翠绿毛边大氅的穆斯林谈得起劲。穿外国装的那位朝人群锐利一扫，看清他们在干什么，拽着那讼师匆匆走了。
「丁·穆罕默德！」萨义德在他们背后嘲弄地喊。「瞧，俺们照你的话办了，埃芬迪！」
「丁·穆罕默德——安拉！安拉！叫不信教的死绝！」几个同伴跟着喊；大半却哑了，像怕。
「那是莫斯科领事的通译。」一个丧气地啼咕。「他跟俺熟，知道俺是谁家的儿子。他准去告俺们的状，俺们今天这一场要坐牢。」
「追上去宰了他！」另一个喊——白骂了一早晨人没挨揍，胆气壮了。
「俺们各回各家、悄悄散了吧！」第三个央告；他叫通译认得太熟，有理由发慌。
他的主意众人听着都好，当场就散了。

===Original===

Saïd went to the coffeehouse of Abu Khalìl, where he smoked a narghileh. The tale of his morning’s pastime made the fat taverner quake with inward laughter. Camr-ud-dìn and his mother stopped work to listen; the customers applauded it as a merry jest. He was obliged to repeat it from the beginning for every newcomer. At midday he made a hearty meal of lentils and bread, drank a cup of coffee, and disposed himself for a nap.
About the second hour after noon he was roused by a strong hand on his shoulder shaking him. To the first blurred glance of his sleepy eyes the whole tavern seemed full of soldiers; but when he sat up he found there were but four of them.
“A scar on his forehead,” one was saying, as if he read over a description in writing, “the beard black, tall and robust, the son of perhaps twenty-three years, his raiment striped of blue and yellow, soiled. This is the man, by Allah!﻿ ﻿… Arise, O my uncle, and come along with us!”
“What means this? What evil have I done?” Saïd rubbed his eyes and stared aghast.
“Who said thou hadst done any wrong? Not I, by Allah! To my mind thou didst well to spit upon the infidels; would to Allah thou hadst slain a few of them! But it is the Wâly’s order that thou go to prison. Make haste, O lazy one!”
Saïd was dimly aware of Abu Khalìl quaking and wringing his hands somewhere between him and the sunlight, of the voices of Camr-ud-dìn and his mother mingled in curses upon the soldiers and their ancestry. Then he was led out into the white glare of the street, where a small crowd of idlers and ne’er-do-wells gaped upon him, and ran along with his captors as an additional escort.
It was clear that the guards had orders to avoid all crowded thoroughfares, for they hurried him through dark tunnels and passages and along mean alleys of an evil savour. But with all these precautions they were obliged to cross the open space before a large khan at an hour when traffic was at its height; and such a group was sure to attract notice, even without the little crowd which followed it implicitly as the tail the dog. The person of the prisoner was much scrutinised, and questions were put to the soldiers, who answered with an “Allah knows!” and a surly shrug. All at once a well-known plaint struck Saïd’s ear.
“Allah will give to you!﻿ ﻿… For the love of Allah, take pity or I die!﻿ ﻿… O Lord!﻿ ﻿… Allah will give to you!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

萨义德去了阿布·哈利勒的咖啡店，抽上一袋水烟。他早晨那场消遣，讲得肥店主肠子笑断。卡姆鲁丁和他娘摺下活计来听；客人们齐声夸这是一桩好笑谈。每来一个新客，他都得从头再讲一遍。晌午，他美美吃了一顿扁豆加面包，喝了一杯咖啡，安排下歇晌。
午后约莫第二个钟头，一只有力的手摇着他的肩膀，把他摇醒。睡眼第一瞥模模糊糊：满店堂都是兵；坐起来一数，统共四个。
「额头一道疤，」一个正念着，像照一张单子，「黑胡子，高个结实，年纪约莫二十三，衣裳蓝黄条纹，带油泥。就是这个人，凭安拉说！……起来，俺的叔，跟俺们走一趟！」
「这是什么意思？俺做了什么恶？」萨义德揉着眼，瞪得发懵。
「谁说你做恶了？俺可没说，凭安拉！依俺看，你啐异教徒啐得好；巴不得你宰了几个！可这是瓦利的令，叫你坐牢。快着点儿，懒骨头！」
萨义德迷迷糊糊觉出阿布·哈利勒在他和日光之间的什么地方哆嗦、搓手，觉出卡姆鲁丁和他娘的嗓音混成一片，咒着兵和兵的祖宗。随后他被带到街面的白光里，一小撮闲汉和无赖朝他张着嘴，跟押他的人一路小跑，白饶上一支护卫。
看得出，这队兵奉了令，专避人多的通衢：他们押着他钻黑洞洞的地道和过道，走一条条气味难闻的陋巷。可饶是这样提防，他们也不得不在一家大客店门前的空场上横穿一回——那正是车马顶闹的时辰；这样一伙人马想不惹眼也难，何况后头还缀着那一小撮人，像狗尾巴一样甩不掉。囚徒叫人相了个够，问题朝兵们抛去，兵们回一句「安拉才知道」，加一个没好气的耸肩。忽然，一个熟悉的哀声扎进萨义德的耳朵。
「安拉会赏你们的！……看在安拉面上，行行好，不然俺就死了！……主啊！……安拉会赏你们的！……」

===Original===

He started, and then howled “Mustafa!” with all the strength of his lungs.
“Hold thy peace, O fool, lest I strike thee on the mouth!” hissed the chief of his escort fiercely.
But the old beggar had heard his cry. The crowd parted suddenly, giving way to a wild, lean figure aflutter with rags. Mustafa raised hands and eyes to Heaven for horror of what he saw.
“What is this?” he shrieked. “Allah cut short their lives! They have taken my son﻿—the staff of my days!﻿—the light of my eyes!﻿ ﻿… These sons of iniquity have robbed me of my son!﻿ ﻿… O Allah!﻿ ﻿… O Lord!﻿ ﻿… O men of Es-Shâm﻿—O fathers of kindness, will you suffer this great wrong to be done in your sight? By the Prophet, there is no sin in him!﻿ ﻿… O Lord!﻿ ﻿… He was ever been a good son and a pious. Say, O Saïd, for what cause have they taken thee and bound thy hands? Let all men judge of thy innocence!”
“For the cause that I cursed the heathen!” shouted Saïd, at the cost of a smart blow on the mouth, which made his gums bleed.
“O Lord!” screamed the old beggar, dancing and rending his clothes as one gone mad with grief. “See, they strike him! There is blood on his lips!﻿ ﻿… They side with unbelievers!﻿ ﻿… They buffet the champion of Islâm and lead him to prison!﻿ ﻿… O men of Es-Shâm, O faithful people, you have heard his crime from his own mouth!﻿ ﻿… O Lord!﻿ ﻿… Rescue him!﻿—rescue my son!﻿—my only son!﻿—the staff of my life!”
The soldiers and their charge were at a standstill, a crowd pressing upon them from every side. There was a sound of muttered curses on all hands, and the shrieks of the old maniac seemed ominous to the guardians of law and order.
“Bah! it is nothing,” shouted the chief of the party so as to be heard afar. “He will be rebuked and lie idle in gaol for a few hours.﻿ ﻿… By Allah, we are no infidels but true men. That old rogue there lies when he says that we side with the Nazarenes. Allah be my witness, it is a lie! But the Wâly’s order is upon us, which to hear is to obey, and those who dare to resist us do so at the risk of heavy punishment.﻿ ﻿… *Oäh! Oäh!* In the name of the Sultàn, make way, I say!”
By soft speaking, mingled deftly with threats, he managed to force a path through the press. In the quiet alley into which they plunged directly he cursed Saïd for a madman and threatened him with every kind of torment as the guerdon of his misbehaviour.

===Chinese===

他一激灵，随后拿出全副肺力嚎了一声「穆斯塔法！」
「闭嘴，蠢货，不然俺抽你的嘴！」押送的头目恶狠狠地嘘他。
可老叫花子听见了那一声。人群豁地分开，让进一个狂乱的瘦影，破布条乱飞。穆斯塔法望见眼前的光景，双手双眼一起朝天。
「这是干什么？」他尖叫。「叫安拉收了他们的命！他们抓了俺的儿子——俺的拐棍！——俺眼里的光！……这帮孽障抢走了俺的儿子！……安拉啊！……主啊！……沙姆的人啊——善心的人们，你们就眼睁睁看着这大冤枉办在你们眼前？凭先知说，他一身无罪！……主啊！……他从来是个孝顺儿子、敬虔人。你说，萨义德，他们为什么抓你、绑你的手？叫众人评评你的清白！」
「为俺咒了野人！」萨义德高喊——嘴上结结实实挨了一记，牙花子打出了血。
「主啊！」老叫花子尖叫，又蹦又撕衣裳，像悲疯了。「瞧啊，他们打他！他嘴上有血！……他们帮异教徒！……他们打伊斯兰的好汉，押他去坐牢！……沙姆的人啊，信士们，他的罪名你们亲耳听见了！……主啊！……救他！——救俺的儿子！——俺独一个的儿子！——俺的拐棍！」
兵和他们的差使困在原地，人群四面压上来。四下里一片压着嗓子的咒骂，老疯子这声声尖叫，在治安的看护人耳朵里听来兆头不祥。
「呸！没大事，」那一伙的头目放声喊，好叫远处都听见。「说他几句，叫他在牢里闲躺几个钟头。……凭安拉说，俺们不是异教徒，是正经人。那老泼皮说俺们帮拿撒勒教徒，是放屁。安拉给俺作证，是瞎话！可瓦利的令押着俺们，听了就得办；谁敢拦俺们，拦了自受重罚。……哇赫！哇赫！奉素丹的名，让路，俺说！」
他软硬兼施、配比得当，硬从人堆里开出一条道。一钻进随后的僻巷，他就骂萨义德是疯子，拿百般刑罚恫吓他，算给他这场胡闹的赏钱。

===Original===

There was peace again, and the soldiers were able to breathe freely. They waxed courageous and blustered as Saïd became sullen and crestfallen. But the old beggar had joined the faithful few who clung to them through all vicissitudes of the road; and he ceased not to revile and execrate them, imploring Allah to strike them all dead and so release his son, until he had watched Saïd disappear within the gate of the prison. Then he sped fleet-foot to the vault of Nûr, to take counsel what was next to be done.

===Chinese===

又太平了，兵们这才敢匀开呼吸。见萨义德蔫了、塌了架，他们的胆气又涨起来，咋咋呼呼。可老叫花子已经入了那一小撮死忠——一路上不管风晴雨雪都缀着不放；他骂不绝口、咒不绝口，求安拉把他们一个个劈死、好放他的儿子，一直骂到亲眼看着萨义德没进监狱的大门。随后他飞起一双快脚，直奔努尔的地窖，去合计下一步怎么办。

### XXII / 二十二
===Original===

Saïd’s first impression of the gaol would have been a pleasant one but for the dejected looks of its inmates and the foul stench pervading its atmosphere. His captors left him unshackled in an open quadrangle. An arcade supporting a flat roof made a sort of verandah on two sides of it, affording shelter to the prisoners from the glare of noon. The remainder was shut in by a high wall, in which was the entrance gate, strongly barred and further secured by a small guard of soldiers hardly less wretched in appearance than the criminals themselves. On one hand the rays of the sinking sun were warm upon wall and pavement; on the other, a deep blue shadow stretched out from the arcade before mentioned almost to the middle of the court.
Saïd stood for some time where his escort had left him, just within the gate. His eyes strayed over the various groups lying or squatting in the shade or striding wearily up and down in the red glow that dyed the eastern wall. Most of them were ragged; all were dirty, with the exception of three young men, who sat aloof together, cross-legged, on the edge of the sunlight. The gaiety of this little party, talking and laughing bravely in the face of misfortune, attracted Saïd even before he knew them for his associates in transgression. His approach was hailed with shouts of welcome, and he was made to sit down with them.
They affected to treat their imprisonment as a jest. It was not likely, Saïd agreed, that men would be greatly punished for so slight a misdemeanour. The Wâly was a Muslim, and all believers must surely feel with them. Their arrest was only a sop to the Franks. That dragoman﻿—curse his religion!﻿—had complained to the Muscovite Consul, his master; and the Consul had gone in a rage to Ahmed Pasha, who was ever ready to humour a Frank in small matters. The Consul’s word was law: the ringleaders were put in prison. On the morrow they would be brought before a council of true believers, gently reprimanded and set at liberty.
Thanks to these assurances, and a good supper which a soldier gladly brought in for them from a neighbouring tavern, Saïd slept well enough that night, though on the bare stones. He had no money to procure bedding such as his friends obtained from the gaolers for a trifle of bakshìsh. But having supped well at their expense, and being used to rough couches, he scarcely envied them the luxury.

===Chinese===

要不是囚徒们那副丧气相和满院秽臭，监狱给赛义德的第一印象本可以算愉快。押他来的人给他不上镣铐，搜在一方露天的四合院里。一道撜着平顶的拱廊，在院子两侧形成游廊，给囚徒们遮正午的烈日。其余两面围以高墙，墙上是大门，门上加着粗闩，还派一小队兵把守——那队兵的模样，比罪犯们好不到哪儿去。一面，西沉的日头把墙和地面晒得暖暖的；另一面，一道深蓝的影子从方才说的那道拱廊里伸出来，几乎伸到院子当中。
押送的人把他搜在大门里侧，他在那儿站了好一阵。他的眼睛游过一簇簇的人：有的躺在荫里，有的蹲着，有的在那染红了东墙的红光里疲惫地蹶来蹶去。多数衣衫褴褛，个个腕臜，只有三个青年例外——他们不合群地坐在一处的日光边上，盘着腿。这一小伙人临着厄运还谈笑自若，那股快活动儿先就把赛义德吸引了——那时他还不知道他们是自己的同案犯。他一凑近，就被欢呼着迎住，被按着同他们坐在了一处。
他们摆出一副把坐牢当玩笑的架势。这样轻的过失，不至于重罚，赛义德也以为然。瓦利是穆斯林，凡信士必同他们同心。抓他们不过是给弗兰克人的一碗安神汤。那个通译——咒他的教门！——向他主子莫斯科领事告了状；领事怒气冲冲找了艾哈迈德帕夏——帕夏在小事上向来惯着弗兰克人。领事的话就是律法：为首的下了狱。明天他们就会被提到一坐真信士面前，轻责几句，开释完事。
托这些宽慰话的福，又托一个大兵欢天喜地从隔壁酒馆替他们买来的一顿好晚饭的福，赛义德那夜虽睡的光石头，倒也睡得够沉。他没钱弄铺盖——朋友们花几个小钱就能从狱卒手里弄到。可他白吃了他们一顿饱的，又睡惯了硬板，那点享受他也不怎么眼馋。

===Original===

He awoke in gladness to the prospect of a speedy release. But the day wore on, and the little company sat ever in the shadow of the arcade, gazing at the gate until their eyes ached. They murmured and grew despondent; darkness returned and they were still in durance. Saïd slept ill that night; his companions moaned and stirred uneasily in their sleep. They were forgotten, or the Franks had poisoned the Wâly’s mind against them. In either case they had small cause to rejoice.
About sunrise, Saïd was awakened by the clank of an iron chain. A peevish voice bade him arise and that quickly. He scrambled to his feet and looked for his companions. They were standing a little way off, under a strong guard of soldiers. Their limbs were fettered, and they were linked together by a heavy chain. He read blank dismay in their faces.
“What is this? What have we done to deserve such usage?” he asked indignantly, as two men, detached for that purpose, fitted irons to his wrists and ankles. There was no answer; the men seemed morose yet handled him gently. Upon his repeating the question in a louder tone the officer in command, who appeared in a towering rage, turned on him fiercely.
“Thou mayst well ask what is this! I myself know not the meaning of it! Perhaps the Wâly is possessed with a devil﻿—Allah knows! To hear is to obey; but to carry out such an order is a shame for one who is a Muslim. May all the Franks perish utterly!﻿ ﻿… Know that the dragoman of the Muscovite Consul﻿—a Christian and the son of an Arab, may his house be destroyed!﻿—was closeted with his Excellency yesterday afternoon. And a little later I received the order for your punishment; that you are to sweep the streets of the Christian quarter in chains. Allah witness, I count it a sin and dishonour to the Faith. Notwithstanding, to hear is to obey!”
He turned aside with a shrug to give a word of command to one of his men. Four common brooms were brought and distributed one to each of the convicts. Saïd was coupled on to the chain with the others, and thus bound together they were marched out at the gate, while every prisoner that was a Muslim ground his teeth and howled with rage at the indignity. The ragged privates who kept the door murmured together with lowering brows.

===Chinese===

他醒来时欢欢喜喜，只等早早开释。可这一天磨啊磨，一小伙人枯坐在拱廊荫里，眼睛把大门盯得发酸。他们嘨咕起来，心灰了；黑暗又回来了，他们还在押。那夜赛义德睡得不好；同案的睡着还哼哼，翻来覆去。他们是被忘了，还是弗兰克人往瓦利耳朵里灌了毒。哪一样都值不得欢喜。
约摸日出，一串铁链的哐啷把赛义德惊醒。一个烦躁的嗓音叫他起来，快些。他手脚并用爬起来，找他的同案。他们站在不远处，重兵看守着。四肢上了镣，一条重链把他们串在一起。他读出他们脸上那种全然的惶骇。
「这是干什么？我们做了什么，受这等待遇？」两个专差过来给他腕上踝上戴镣，他愤愤地问。没人答话；那两人闷着脸，手上却轻。他把问题提高嗓门又问一遍，带队的军官——正憋着一腔雷霆——猛地转向他。
「你问得对，这是干什么！我自己也不明白！兴许瓦利叫鬼迷了——安拉知道！听命得遵；可这样的差事，叫一个穆斯林来办，是耻辱。叫弗兰克人死绝了吧！……你可知道，那莫斯科领事的通译——一个基督徒，又是阿拉伯人的儿子，叫他房子塌了！——昨天下午同大人闭门密谈。过后不久我就接到惩处你们的命令：给你们戴上链子，去扫基督徒区的街。安拉作证，我认这是罪过，是教门的耻辱。话虽如此，听命得遵！」
他耸耸肩闪过一边，朝手下吩咐了一句。四把寻常扫帚抱了来，罪犯一人一把。赛义德给串上链子，同其余的锁作一串，就这么拴着押出了大门；凡是穆斯林的囚徒，个个咬牙切齿，为这羞辱嚎出怒来。看门的褴褛大兵们也眉头拧紧，交头接耳。

===Original===

“Jurji, the Nazarene, that was a malefactor, was set free without punishment,” Saïd heard one of them growl; “while these believers, who have done nothing to be called a crime, are condemned to dishonour the Faith. In truth, the end of all things is at hand!”
Their road lay past the gateway of the great mosque. The sight of the white minaret with its crescent glittering upon the blue brought scalding tears to men’s eyes for the honour of Islâm which was dead. The cooing of the doves had a new and mournful note in it. The prisoners walked listless with downcast faces; the soldiers closed in to screen them, as far as might be, from the stare of the populace. But the guard themselves were sullen and dejected; the work in hand being a heavy burden on their minds. Suddenly a piercing cry broke upon the hush in which they moved.
“O Lord!﻿ ﻿… I behold my son﻿—my only son﻿—the staff of my age﻿—whom the children of sin took from me! The slaves of iniquity have loaded him with chains﻿—Allah, cut short their lives!﻿ ﻿… By the Quran, he is no evildoer, but a pious man and a faithful﻿—who did but curse some Nazarenes and spit in their faces. It is for that they have fettered and bound him!﻿ ﻿… O Lord!﻿ ﻿… Shall these things be done under the sun and in the sight of all men? Merciful Allah!”
The soldiers quickened step, but the voice went along with them, as it were a knife stabbing their hearts, which were sore enough already. Why did not the sun veil his face and spread a darkness over all the city that the shame of Islâm might be hid? Oh, that Allah would cause the earth to yawn and swallow up the infidels, as he did for Neby Mûsa of old; that all the world might know that God was still watching over his faithful as in the time of Nûh and Ibrahìm and Ismaìl, as in the days of Daûd and of Isa, and of Muhammed (peace to him!), his apostle. O day of woe! O cursed day of infamy!
That was a proud morning for the Christians. They swarmed in the streets of their quarter with exultant faces. The day of their deliverance was come at last. The conquerers were become the slaves of the conquered, to sweep their streets for them. They gloated on the sight with the coward’s triumph, who, seeing his foe laid low by a stronger than himself, spits valiantly in his face and cries, “Mine is the victory!”

===Chinese===

「朱尔吉那拿撒勒人，本是个作恶的，放了，一个子儿的罚没受，」赛义德听见其中一个咕哝；「这几个信士，没做一桩够得上叫罪的，倒判了教门的羞辱。说真的，末日的日子到了！」
他们走的路要过大清真寺的门。白宣礼塔的新月衬在蓝天上闪光——这一眼叫人眼眶滚烫，为那已死的伊斯兰的体面。鸽子的咕哝里也添了新调子，哀哀的。囚徒们垂头丧气地走；士兵们围拢来，尽力挡开众人的注视。可看守自己也是一脸阴沉沮丧，手里这差事沉甸甸压在心上。忽然，一声尖利的呼号刺破他们行走其中的那片肃静。
「主啊！……我望见我的儿——我独一的儿——我老年的拐杖——罪恶之子们把他从我手里夺去了！不义的奴才们给他戴上了链子——安拉，折他们的寿！……凭古兰经说，他不是作恶的，是个虔诚人、忠实人——不过咒了几个拿撒勒人、唾了他们一脸。就为这个，他们给他上了镣、加了锁！……主啊！……这样的事，可以在日头底下、在众人眼前做么？仁慈的安拉！」
士兵们加快了步子，可那嗓音一路随着，像一把刀，扎他们本来就疼的心。太阳怎么不掩上面、给全城铺下一片黑，好遮住伊斯兰的耻？哦，安拉怎么不叫大地张口，把不信教者吞了——像古时为先知穆萨做的那样；叫普天下知道，真主仍在看顾祂的信士，如在努哈、易卜拉欣、伊斯玛仪的日子，如在达乌德、尔萨、并祂的使者穆罕默德（愿他平安！）的日子。苦日！受咒的奇耻大辱之日！
对基督徒，那是个扬眉吐气的早晨。他们欢天喜地，成群涌在自家地界的街上。他们翻身的日子到底来了。征服者做了被征服者的奴才，替他们扫街。他们饱看这光景，怀着懦夫的凯旋——懦夫见对头叫比自己强的人打翻在地，便奋勇地朝那张脸啐一口，喊：「胜利是我的！」

===Original===

Secure of protection, they took pleasure in taunting the prisoners, cursing them for sons of dogs and mocking them with proffers of water when they seemed weary. The pent-up venom of centuries was on their tongues. The poor earthworm hissed like a snake.
A number of the faithful had flocked into the quarter, drawn chiefly by the frantic outcry of the old beggar. They failed at first to grasp the position. The valorous attitude of the Christians only shocked and bewildered them. But no sooner did they learn what work was doing than their eyes grew fierce with the old pride of Islâm﻿—the battle-pride of their forbears, who had carried the white crescent on the green flag victorious from India to the Atlantic. There were scuffles, and Christians were hurled to the ground. The press grew menacing about the sweepers and their guard. The soldiers looked anxious. The prisoners were ordered to cease work, and the officer, foreseeing a riot, was minded to take them back to prison on his own responsibility. The courage of the Nazarenes began to waver. The older and wiser of them slipped quietly into the nearest houses. But the younger and more turbulent, loth to forego one tittle of the unwonted pleasure of retaliation, remained in the street, hurling insults at the religion of Muhammed, and all professing it. Even thus they outnumbered the believers, who, however, were constantly on the increase as the rumour of a tumult spread through the city. In vain did the captain attempt to draw off his men, for they were locked in the heart of a seething, yelling crowd. It was all they could do to hold their ground. All at once the voice of the old beggar was raised in triumph﻿—
“To the rescue!﻿—Dìn! Dìn! Dìn Muhammed!”
There was a rush of turbaned men, a sharp struggle; the soldiers were torn away like trees by a winter torrent, and a hundred hands were eager to free the prisoners from their fetters. Files, knives, iron bars﻿—every kind of tool and weapon was thrust forward to serve in the work of release. Rescuers and rescued were rocked to and fro in the battle raging around them. For once the Christians fought like wild beasts. Here a turbaned head was seen to fall, there a fez. Death shrieks mingled with the howls and shouts of the fighters. The uproar was frightful. For a while the issue of the fray seemed doubtful; but soon the Christians began to give way.

===Chinese===

有庇护壮胆，他们以嘲弄囚徒为乐，骂他们是狗崽子，看他们乏了，就拿水递到跟前取笑。积了几百年的毒，都在舌头上放了。可怜的蚯蚓也嘶嘶地做起蛇来。
一批信士涌进了那片地界，多半是叫老叫花子那声嘶力竭的哭喊引来的。起初他们摸不清局面。基督徒那副胆壮的架势只是把他们惊了、懵了。可一打听明白在干什么活，他们的眼睛便凶起来，燃起伊斯兰古老的骄傲——先祖的战魂，当年搜着绿旗上的白新月，从印度一路凯旋到大西洋。推搡起来了，基督徒被搜倒在地。人群朝扫街的和看守步步进逼。士兵们面有惧色。囚徒被喝令停工；军官料着要出乱子，打算自己做主把人押回牢去。拿撒勒人的胆气开始动摇。年长些、精明些的悄悄溜进了最近的宅门。可年轻气盛的那伙，舍不得这一星半点难得的报复甜头，赖在街上，朝穆罕默德的教门、朝教门的信众泼脏话。即便这样他们人数还占上风——只是风声传遍全城，信士们一刻多似一刻。队长想把队伍拉出去，白费劲：他们锁死在一片沸腾叫喊的人海心子里。能站住脚已是竭尽全力。冷不防，老叫花子的嗓音得胜般地扬起——
「救人哪！——教门！教门！穆罕默德的教门！」
一片缠头巾的人潮涌来，一场短兵相接；士兵们像树被冬洪连根拔走，一百只手抢着解囚徒的镣铐。锉刀、尖刀、铁条——各式家伙一齐递上来效力。救人的和被救的在四周的酣战里荡来荡去。基督徒这一回倒打得像野兽。这边一颗缠头巾的头落地，那边一顶菲斯帽。惨叫混在斗士们的嚎吼里。鼎沸得怕人。有一阵胜负像是难说；可基督徒很快就撑不住了。

===Original===

The war-cry of Islâm gathered volume, until it seemed to roll along the sky in waves of sound.
“To the house of Yuhanna!” cried the old beggar, dragging Saïd’s arm. “Dìn Muhammed! to the house of ’hanna, the pig who protects Jurji, the evildoer!﻿ ﻿… Y’Allah!﻿ ﻿… Death to the heathen!”
Saïd, freed of his chains, forced his way earnestly through the crowd. Mustafa dogged him, screaming, laughing, and yelling like one possessed, keeping tight hold of his raiment so as not to lose him. A number of the faithful, fired by the hated name of Jurji, followed frantic as they.

===Chinese===

伊斯兰的战号越滚越响，直到像一波波的声浪沿天推进。
「上尤汉纳家去！」老叫花子拽着赛义德的胳膊喊。「穆罕默德的教门！上汉纳那猪猡家去——那护着恶徒朱尔吉的猪猡！……呀，安拉！……异教徒死！」
赛义德卸了链子，一心往前，从人堆里挤开一条道。穆斯塔法钉着他，又叫又笑，像鬼附体地嚎，死死攋住他的衣裳生怕丢了。一伙信士，叫朱尔吉这仇恨的名字点着了火，跟着他们一路狂奔。

### XXIII / 二十三
===Original===

The house of Yuhanna was at some distance from the scene of riot. Its outer door stood open as on other days, and at the moment when Saïd burst into its pretty court, the girl Ferideh was seated on a cushion in the shade of the lemon-trees, her little brother in her lap. Suddenly, as if the stillness had been some brittle thing, it shivered to a great roar. There was a whirr and a flutter as the pigeons rose in a cloud from their researches on the pavement.
Snatching up the child, she sprang to her feet. The menace of the wild inhuman faces appalled her. She fled towards the door of the house in terror at that inroad of madmen as she deemed them. But the old beggar, outrunning Saïd caught her by the arm and shook her brutally.
“Say, girl, is the pig, thy father, in the house?”
Ferideh winced for the tightness of his grasp. Outraged pride and a certain fearful wonder were blended in her answer.
“Be not so rough, I pray!﻿ ﻿… Know that my father receives no man today, for he lies upon his bed, having fever. Tomorrow he will perhaps be well, and, when well, he is accessible to all who seek him.”
Mustafa laughed aloud, and pushed her so that she staggered backward a few paces.
“He receives no man, sayest thou? By the tomb of the Prophet, he will receive us! Aha, O ’hanna, thou old rat, thou devourer of women, the avenger of blood overtakes thee at last!” He drew a long knife from his girdle and flashed it in the face of the girl.
“Dìn Muhammed!” he cried. “Death to the infidels! Y’Allah!” and rushed into the house, hurling to the ground an old woman, almost blind, who had come to the door seeking querulously to know the meaning of the uproar. The crowd raised a loud shout and pressed after him.
“O holy Miriam! O Yesua, Redeemer of the world, save him, save my father!” shrieked the maiden, falling on her knees, appealing to the sky above, whose bright peace mocked her anguish. The mob, bent on plunder, only laughed at her and praised her looks in passing. She grew white and red by turns, and her lips moved with difficulty as she prayed.
The scared pigeons circled overhead, whirling great flakes of shadow over wall and pavement. Their cooing and the tinkle of the rill from the basin, heard despite the tumult, were heartrending as memories. The still foliage of the lemon-trees cast a dark pool of shadow on the flags.

===Chinese===

尤汉纳的宅子离骚乱的地方有一程。外门照常开着；萨义德冲进那座漂亮院子的一刻，姑娘费丽黛正坐在柠檬树荫里的一只坐垫上，怀里捾着她的小弟弟。忽然，那份寂静像一件脆东西，哗啦碎成一声巨吼。鸽子从地上的觅食中轰然腾起，一片翅响。
她一把抱起孩子，跳起来。那一张张狰狞不似人面的威胁吓坏了她。她朝屋门逃去——在她眼里，这是一群疯子的闯入。可老叫花子抢在萨义德前头，攦住她的胳膊，狠狠摇她。
「说，丫头，那头猪——你爹——在屋里吗？」
他攦得太紧，费丽黛皱起眉。她的答话里掺着受辱的傲气和一种发慌的惊异。
「求你别这么粗鲁！……要知道俺父亲今天不见客，他发烧，卧床呢。明天他兴许就好了；好了，他见一切来访的人。」
穆斯塔法放声大笑，一掌推得她踉跄倒退几步。
「不见客，你说？凭先知的坟说，他见俺们！啊哈，哈纳，你这老耗子，你这吃女人的货，血债的追讨到底追上你了！」他从腰里抽出一把长刀，在姑娘眼前一晃。
「丁·穆罕默德！异教徒死！雅安拉！」他喊着一头冲进屋，把一个几乎瞎了的老妈子撞翻在地——她刚摸到门口，正吵着问外头闹什么。人群一声呐喊，跟着压进去。
「圣米利暗啊！耶苏阿啊，救世的主啊，救他，救俺父亲！」姑娘跪倒在地，朝天哀号；天上那片明晃晃的安宁，嘲弄着她的苦楚。那群只顾掳掠的暴民朝她笑，走过还夸她的模样。她一阵白一阵红，祷告的嘴唇都难得动弹。
受惊的鸽子在头顶绕圈，把大片大片的影子旋过院墙和石地。任凭底下闹翻了天，它们的咕咕声和石盆里细流的叮咚还是钻进耳朵，像回忆一样割人心。柠檬树纹丝不动的叶子，在石板上投下一泓黑潭。

===Original===

The leaves of a creeper on the wall trembled a little.
Saïd made no attempt to enter the house. He had no thirst for blood, no desire for gain. The screams and yells that arose within only confused his brain. He drew near to the kneeling girl, and she did not see him; but the child saw him and clung closer, burying its face in her bosom. He felt bashful﻿—at a loss how to proceed. The court was deserted now; he thought he would have felt bolder in the presence of a crowd. The shouting and the noise, though friendly, numbed his wits. Forgetful for a moment of what was going on within the house, he began to make playful overtures to her baby brother.
Through an open lattice a frightful shriek rent the air, deadening all other sounds. Another, and then another.﻿ ﻿… The girl leapt to her feet and listened, hugging the little one so tight that it cried fretfully.
“O just Allah! they are killing my father!” she cried, and was rushing blindly towards the open door when Saïd caught her in his arms.
“Unhand me, loose me, wild beast! Let me go to my father. Dost hear his cry? They kill him﻿—an old man and sick, lying on his bed with none to help him.”
She fought him frantically for a moment with teeth and feet, always holding the child fast to her breast. Then, as if all her strength were spent, she gave one bitter cry and was still.
Holding her thus in his arms, Saïd felt uplifted beyond all care of life or death. What matter though a hundred old men were butchered if only he could manage to convey her away from that place to the upper chamber of Nûr, the harlot.
“I suffer with thee, O my beloved!” he murmured soothingly. “But thy father was old; the days that remained to him were few in number. Also the people are mad this day against every Nazarene.﻿ ﻿… Listen, pretty one! If they find thee here they will surely slay thee, and this child also. Now I have so great love for thee that I would not let a hair of thy head be harmed. By Allah, I would slay the man who dared to touch thee with a finger! Come with me, O my soul, and I will lead thee to a place of safety.”
She gave no answer nor any sign that she heard, but weighed heavily upon him. Looking down, he realised that she had swooned.
The little boy, escaped from her embrace, was trotting eagerly towards the door of the house, through which rich carpets and other furniture of price were being flung out pell-mell.

===Chinese===

墙上一架藤蔓的叶子微微抖了抖。
萨义德没有进屋。他不渴血，也不图财。屋里腾起的一片尖叫嘶喊只搅得他脑子发懵。他挨近跪着的姑娘；她没看见他，可孩子看见了他，往她怀里贴得更紧，把脸埋进她胸脯。他竟有些害臊——不知下一步怎么迈。院子这会儿空了；他想，人多些他兴许倒胆壮。那片喊杀虽是友军，却叫他发木。一时间他忘了屋里正干什么，逗起她的小弟弟来。
敞开的窗棂里一声惨叫划破长空，压倒一切声音。又一声，又一声。……姑娘跳起来听，把孩子捾得紧到哇哇直哭。
「公道的安拉！他们在杀俺父亲！」她喊，瞎着眼朝那扇敞开的门扑，萨义德一把把她抱住。
「放开俺，松手，野兽！叫俺到俺父亲那儿去。你听见他的喊了吗？他们杀他——一个老人，病着，躺在床上，没人救他。」
她拿牙拿脚疯狂地挣扎了一阵，孩子始终死死箍在胸口。随后，像力气一朝用尽，她苦叫一声，不动了。
这样把她抱在怀里，萨义德觉着自己升到了生死之外。哪怕一百个老人遭屠，有什么要紧——只要他能想法子把她从这地方弄到努尔那妓女楼上的屋里去。
「俺跟你一块儿受苦，俺的心肝！」他柔声哄。「可你父亲年纪大了；留给他的日子本来就数得清。再说，今天全城的人见了拿撒勒教徒就疯。……听着，可人儿！他们要是在这儿撞见你，准连你带这孩子一起杀。俺疼你疼到什么份上——你一根头发也不能叫人碰。凭安拉说，谁敢伸一根指头碰你，俺就宰谁！跟俺来，俺的魂儿，俺领你去个安稳地方。」
她没有答，也没有听见的样子，只是沉沉地坠着他。低头一看，他才知道她晕过去了。
小男孩从她怀里滑脱，正颠着小步子往屋门跑——门里正朝外乱抛地毯和值钱的家具。

===Original===

Saïd, who was fond of children, called to him that there were devils in there, and bade him fly to some neighbour’s house. Whereupon the little fellow toddled for the street in terror of his life.
He had raised the fainting girl in his arms and was bearing her swiftly towards the outer gate, when Mustafa overtook him.
“Aha, thou performest thy part? It is good﻿—very good! Now listen!﻿—I slew him. See, his blood is still warm on my left hand.﻿ ﻿… I was the first to plunge a knife into him; but, before I smote, I made him teach me the place where his treasure lies hid. At my bidding the multitude held their hands and stood back, knowing that I had private cause to hate him. He told me readily, in a whisper, thinking to save his life. But I slew him﻿—with this knife I slew him. It is a good knife﻿—a sharp knife. By Allah, I love this knife as my brother from this day forth. Ha, ha!”
He sank his voice.
“I go now to secure the money. There is a fountain﻿—thou knowest it?﻿—out yonder among the gardens, built on the pattern of a little mosque. In the pavement of its recess is a loose stone covering a hole where I am used to bury trifles. There I will conceal the wealth, and afterwards I will seek thee at the house of Nûr. Make haste, O my son!﻿ ﻿… Look, there is smoke: they set fire to the house!﻿ ﻿… The girl is pretty, and some of them might quarrel with thee for her sake. My peace go with thee!”
Saïd strode out into the street with his burden and plunged into the network of dark passages and byways he had threaded so often for desire of her. He had not gone far before she began to give signs of a return to consciousness. He paused awhile in a secluded place to give her time to recover. Presently, to his great relief, she was able to stand on her feet, though still dazed and needing support for every step. She asked not whither they went, nor seemed to care. Indeed, she evinced no mind or will of her own, but moved wherever he led her, without reluctance as without eagerness. Her beauty, and the strange sight of a Muslim shepherding a Christian maid, caused the men they met to stare at them; so that Saïd, having no wish to court notice, bade her draw the fall of her white hood across her face, as the Drûz women used to do. She obeyed by a vague movement which told that her mind wandered.
Nûr was cooking her noonday meal on the brazier when they entered.

===Chinese===

萨义德喜欢孩子，喎住他，说里头有魔鬼，叫他快上哪家邻舍躲命。小家伙吓得迈开小腿直奔街上去了。
他抱起晕着的姑娘，快步朝外门走，穆斯塔法追了上来。
「啊哈，你的差事办得好？好——好得很！听着！——俺宰了他。瞧，他的血在俺左手上还温着。……头一刀是俺扎进去的；可下刀之前，俺逼他招了他藏宝的地方。俺一句话，众人都缩了手，退开，知道俺跟他有私仇。他招得痛快，贴着耳朵说的，想着留一条命。可俺还是宰了他——拿这把刀宰的。好刀——快刀。凭安拉说，打今儿起，俺爱这把刀赛过亲兄弟。哈哈！」
他压低嗓音。
「俺这就去看住那笔钱。有一座喷泉——你知道吧？——在园子那头，修得像一座小清真寺。它壁龛的地面上有一块活石，底下有个洞，俺惯常埋些零碎。俺把财宝藏那儿，回头上努尔家找你。快着，俺的儿子！……瞧，冒烟了：他们放火烧房了！……这丫头俊，他们里头保不齐有人为你跟她争。俺的平安跟你去！」
萨义德抱着他的重负跨到街上，一头扎进那一带黑洞洞的过道小巷——为着她，他这些路早走熟了。没走多远，她有了还魂的迹象。他在一处僻静地方停了停，给她缓的工夫。不多时，谢天谢地，她能站了，人还发懵，一步一扶。她不问去哪儿，也不像在意。说真的，她整个人没了主意也没了意愿，他领到哪儿走到哪儿，不推也不就。她的姿色，加上一个穆斯林领着一个基督徒姑娘的怪景，引得路人直瞪；萨义德不想招眼，叫她把白兜帽的垂帘拉下来遮脸，像德鲁兹女人那样。她依了，动作虚虚的，看得出神思还在别处飘。
他们进门时，努尔正就着火盆做她的晌午饭。

===Original===

She welcomed Saïd with delight and cast a searching glance at his charge. Then, as he began to explain, she checked him with an impatient gesture and a nod of intelligence. She understood perfectly. He had been sent to sweep the streets of the infidels. Oh, the sin of it! She had heard the news from the son of Abu Khalìl when he brought some figs she had asked of his father. The whole city was ashamed. There had been a riot﻿—not so?﻿—and he had been rescued. And then Mustafa﻿—the old madman!﻿—had led the mob to the house of ’hanna, his enemy. And this then was Saïd’s beloved?
She thrust her painted face close to that pale one and scanned the features narrowly. Then she passed her hands down the loose robe, feeling the limbs beneath.
“She is sweet﻿—a pearl!﻿—a darling!” she exclaimed. “By Allah, thou art in luck’s way, O my soul. Art happy at last?﻿ ﻿… She neither sees nor hears us. Poor love! she is distraught with grief. It happens timely that the upper chamber is ready. I prepared it for the pleasure of a certain effendi, but his girl is a Nazarene and, in these troublous times, will not dare come hither. I will tend her there, the priceless gem! And thou must not come nigh her until the evening. Dost hear, O Saïd? She must sleep and take refreshment, and Nûr will tend her. Wait until the evening, I say; and then, when she is a little rested, I will present thee as her deliverer.”
With that she put an arm round Ferideh’s waist and supported her very tenderly up the flight of steps to the guest-chamber. And Saïd sat on his heels, rolling cigarette after cigarette, drinking glass after glass of rose sherbet, too perturbed to eat though Nûr pressed him to share her repast. And Nûr, for her part, took a malicious joy in his distress, looking forth from time to time from the door of the upper room to wag her head at him and whisper, trumpeting with her hand﻿—
“She is sweet, I tell thee!﻿—white as milk!﻿—a darling! I that am a woman cannot choose but kiss her!”

===Chinese===

她欢欢喜喜迎了萨义德，朝他抱的人锐利一扫。他刚要解释，她一个不耐烦的手势、一个心照不宣的点头截住了他。她全明白。他这是奉命扫大街上的异教徒去了。喎，造孽！阿布·哈利勒的儿子替他爹送她要的无花果时，把消息带给她了。全城丢脸。闹了一场乱子——是不是？——他给放出来了。跟着穆斯塔法——老疯子！——领着人上了他仇人哈纳家。这么说，这位就是萨义德的心肝？
她把她那张画过的脸凑近那张苍白的脸，细细相了一回。又顺着那件宽袍往下摸，掂量底下的身条。
「她可人——一颗珠子！——个小宝贝！」她叫起来。「凭安拉说，你走红运了，俺的心肝。你总算遂了愿？……她瞧不见俺们，也听不见。可怜见儿的，悲痛把魂儿撞散了。楼上那间屋正好现成，来得巧。俺原是伺候一位埃芬迪的兴头收拾的；可他那位姑娘是拿撒勒教徒，这乱糟糟的年月，不敢来了。俺把她安顿在那儿，这颗无价的宝石！天黑以前你可不许近她。听见没有，萨义德？她得睡，得进点饮食，努尔来伺候。等天黑，俺说；等她缓过来一点，俺就把你引见给她，当她的救命恩人。」
说罢她搂住费丽黛的腰，一路搀得温柔，上那挂石梯进了客房。萨义德蹲在脚跟上，一支接一支搓烟卷，一杯接一杯灌玫瑰香露，心乱得吃不下——努尔劝他同桌，他推了。努尔呢，拿他的煎熬当恶意的乐子，不时从楼上屋门口探出头来，朝他晃脑袋，拿手圈着嘴悄声喊——
「她可人，俺告诉你！——白得像奶！——个小宝贝！俺一个做女人的，都忍不住要亲她！」

### XXIV / 二十四
===Original===

The first lilac gloom of night had fallen on the city ere the old beggar regained the vault of Nûr. A feeble glow from the brazier showed his wrinkled face ghastly pale and distorted with nervous twitchings. Madness burned in his eyes. His fingers clenched and unclenched spasmodically; his staff fell from them with a thud upon the earthern floor.
“O Nûr, hear me! Where art thou?” he cried, peering about in the darkness. “I have slain him, I tell thee﻿—I have slain the pig ’hanna﻿—the enemy of my house.﻿ ﻿…”
“Hist!﻿—Hold thy peace!” The door of the upper chamber was opened and shut. There was the rustle of a dress and clank of trinkets as the old woman came down the steps. “She is up there: his daughter, dost understand? Saïd has been with her, but against my advice he was violent and frightened her. She fought like a tigress and screamed so that I had to interfere. By my head, it is lucky that my house is a place apart, walled off and secluded, else all the quarter must have come together, seeking the cause of her outcry. For long I have been trying to soothe her; now at length she is silent and I am glad of it. As for Saïd, she has scratched and bitten him finely. A little while since he went out to gather tidings; he will return presently. Now sit down, O my uncle, and I will warm up thy supper, which was ready long ago.”
But Mustafa gave no heed to what she said. Except that he lowered his voice somewhat it seemed that he heard nothing of it. Clutching her arm, he launched into a sort of chant of praise and thanksgiving.
“Allah is bountiful!﻿ ﻿… I slew him, I tell thee! He lay on his bed shamming sickness; and I held the rage of the faithful in check till he had whispered me the secret of his treasure. He thought to preserve his life thereby, deeming we were come to rob him. But I spoke the word, I called on the name of Allah! I shouted in his ear the name of the girl, my sister, whom he ruined. A hundred knives struck down at him as he lay; but mine was foremost and it cut his life.﻿ ﻿… Praise to Allah!
“Ha, ha! He was fat and lay on a soft bed, whereas I am lean and used to sleep on the earth. Yet I slew him!﻿ ﻿… See the stains on my left hand﻿—O hand of honour, O blessed hand!﻿ ﻿… The fat who dwell in palaces must reckon with the lean beggar at their gates. I would, O Nûr, thou hadst seen him in the death-throe. He looked so funny that all men laughed.

===Chinese===

夜里头一层丁香色的幽暗落满城时，老叫花子才回到努尔的地窖。火盆一点微光映出他皱纹纵横的脸：白得吓人，抽搦得走了形。疯癫在他眼里烧着。手指一阵阵痉挛地攋紧又张开；手杖从指间脱手，喍地砸在泥地上。
「努尔，听我说！你在哪儿？」他喊，在黑暗里四下张望。「我杀了他，告诉你——我杀了那猪猡汉纳——我家的仇敌。……」
「嘘！——闭嘴！」阁楼的门开了又关。老太婆下楼来，衣衫窸窣，环佩叮当。「她在上头：他女儿，明白么？赛义德守过她，可他不听我劝，动蛮的，吓着她了。她斗得像头母虎，叫得那样凶，我只得出头。拿我的脑袋起誓，亏得我这宅子独门独院、墙高路僻，不然四邻早围拢来问她嚎什么了。我哄了她这半天，这会儿总算静了，我也放了心。至于赛义德，叫她抓的咬的够呛。他刚出去打听消息，这就回来。坐下吧，伯伯，我给你把晚饭热热——早就得了。」
穆斯塔法却没听她说。除了把嗓音压低了几分，那些话像是全没进他耳朵。他攋住她的胳膊，唱起了一段赞美与谢恩的调子。
「安拉大能！……我杀了他，告诉你！他躺在床上装病；我按着信士们的怒火，等他把宝藏的秘密咬了耳朵。他当这样能保命，只当我们是来劫财的。可我说出了那句话，我呼唤了安拉的名！我在他耳边喊出那姑娘的名字——我妹妹，被他糟蹋了的。一百把刀一齐朝躺着的他剁下去；可我的刀最先，割断了他的命。……赞美安拉！
「哈，哈！他肥，躺在软床上；我瘦，睡惯了泥地。到底是我杀了他！……瞧我这左手上的血渍——光荣的手，有福的手！……住宫殿的肥人，总得同他们门前的瘦叫花子算账。努尔，你真该看看他断气的样子。他那模样滑稽得人人都笑了。

===Original===

Ha, ha, ha!﻿ ﻿… Thanks be to Allah! The reproach is taken away from my father’s house. Allah is gracious!”
“Thou art overwrought, O father of Mansûr,” she said soothingly. “Sit down and rest. See, thy supper is ready!﻿ ﻿… By Allah, thou art very old for this work, and I fear lest it prove harmful to thy health. Sit down, dost hear me? After a little while Saïd will return and we shall learn what news there is. In the meantime I will make some coffee for thee.”
The old beggar allowed himself to be persuaded. He sank down cross-legged by the threshold of the inner room, while she, having made fast the door, shook an earthen lamp to be sure it had oil enough, lit and set it in a hollow nook of the wall opposite to him. By its light she observed him furtively as she busied herself about the brazier, and she shook her head bodingly from time to time. A torn strip of his filthy turban dangled over one ear. His scanty robe, all ragged, displayed the thick growth of grizzled hair upon his chest. His bare limbs were shrivelled and sinewy, of the colour of a sun-dried apricot, the legs dusty almost to the knee. His withered hand was extended as when he sat by the wayside for alms.
It was as if mere change of posture had been a charm to quench his excitement. The life was gone from his limbs, the fire from his eyes. He was become bowed and very feeble﻿—an old, old man whose hours are numbered. His mouth hung open slavering. The under lip moved perpetually us he gurgled certain phrases, always the same, seeming catchwords to something he would fain recall.
“Allah is bountiful.﻿ ﻿… I slew him.﻿ ﻿… Dìn Muhammed.﻿ ﻿… O blessed left hand.﻿ ﻿… Allah is bountiful!﻿ ﻿…”
Nûr shook him with rough kindness as she set a smoking bowl of chopped meat and rice at his knees with the charge to wake up and eat. She held the dish under his nostrils that the savoury steam might beget a craving. She grew poetical in praise of its contents; but all in vain.
Mechanically he thrust a trembling hand into the mess and raised a portion to his mouth; but he let the rice slip through his fingers without so much as licking them.
Nûr was greatly concerned. He must be on the brink of death, she told herself, thus to neglect good victuals, he who was always wont to come in ravenous from a day’s begging.

===Chinese===

哈，哈，哈！……感谢安拉！我父家门上的耻辱除掉了。安拉仁慈！」
「你这是过耗了，曼苏尔的爹，」她宽慰道。「坐下歇歇。瞧，你的晚饭得了！……凭安拉说，干这行你太老了，我怕它伤你的元气。坐下，听见没有？过一会儿赛义德就回来，咱们听听有什么信儿。这会儿我给你煮咖啡。」
老叫花子由她劝住了。他盘腿颓坐在里间门槛边；她闩死了门，摇了摇一盏陶灯看油够不够，点上，搁在他对面墙上一个凹龛里。她就着灯光，一边守着火盆忙，一边偷眼觑他，时不时预感不祥地摇一摇头。他那条脏头巾撕烂的一角，吊在一只耳朵上。单薄的袍子处处挂缕，露出胸口一片花白的浓毛。赤裸的四肢干瘦多筋，颜色像晒干的杏子，两条腿蒙尘直蒙到膝盖。那只枯手朝前伸着，同他坐在路边讨钱时一个姿势。
仿佛单单换个姿势，就是一道镇住他亢奋的符。生气从四肢里走了，火从眼睛里走了。他弯了，萎了——一个寿数已屈指的老而又老的人。嘴张着，淌着涎。下唇不住地嚅动，咕哝着几句话，永远是那几句，像是一段他想不起来的事的切口。
「安拉大能。……我杀了他。……穆罕默德的教门。……有福的左手啊。……安拉大能！……」
努尔把一碗冒着热气的碎肉烑饭搁在他膝头，粗声粗气却好心好意地摇他，叫醒他吃。她把碗举到他鼻子底下，叫喷香的热气勾出馋来。她夸起碗里的内容，简直带了诗兴；全是白费。他机械地伸出一只颤手插进饭里，舀起一捧送到嘴边；饭却从指缝里漏光了，他连指头都不舐一下。
努尔着了大慌。这样好饭都不碰，他准是到了死门边了——她暗想——一个天天讨完饭回来饿虎似的男人。

===Original===

She made shift to feed him with her own hands and rejoiced to find that he swallowed the morsels placed in his mouth.
While she was thus occupied the door was tried from without. A knocking ensued, and the voice of Saïd calling to her to open. She left her charge and flew to shoot back the bolt.
“Where is Mustafa?﻿ ﻿… Bid him come away with all speed! It is said that search is made for us for our part in the destruction of Yuhanna’s house. Ah, there he is! Rise, O my father, and come with me. The carnage of this day is nothing compared with what tomorrow’s sun will see. Know that a great multitude of Christians, fugitives from the Mountain, have entered the city seeking refuge. And many Drûz, both from the Mountain and the Hauran, have pursued them hither. I met a party of them in this minute as I came through the streets. They are strong men of war and armed like soldiers. They are eager as ourselves against the pagans.﻿ ﻿… Arise, O Mustafa, and come away! It is known that we frequent this place, and it were a shame to be taken a prisoner on the eve of so great a festival.﻿ ﻿… Arise, I say! What ails thee? Art ill? Speak! What is this, O Nûr?”
The woman clung to his arm.
“Merciful Allah! I fear he is at the point to die. At his first coming he was as one possessed, shouting and screaming and waving his hands. It was very hard for me to quiet him. Now he is like one in a swoon; he sees me not nor hears me, and is weaker than a baby.”
“I warrant he is only tired. If Allah will I shall find means to rouse him. He is as my father, and this place is dangerous for him.”
He strode to the place where Mustafa sat cross-legged, mumbling fragments of sentences, and staring at the basin of rice and meat. He grasped the old man’s shoulder and bent over him, raising his voice as if to overtake the wandering mind and call it back.
“Fie upon thee, O my father!” he cried, “thou who hast this day slain the enemy with thy own hand, and hast done battle so bravely for the Faith, to sicken and faint like a vaporous girl. Allah witness I am ashamed for thee! Awake, O Mustafa! This place is not safe for us. The soldiers﻿—Allah blast them!﻿—may be seeking us even now. If we stay here we shall be taken and put in prison, and must forego all the glory of tomorrow’s slaughter. The wrath of Islâm burns like a great fire to consume the infidels.

===Chinese===

她索性亲手喂他，见他张嘴咽下那一口口饭，心里欢喜。
正忙着，门外有人拧门。跟着是敲击，是赛义德叫她开门的嗓音。她搜下手里的活儿，抢过去抽闩。
「穆斯塔法在哪儿？……叫他赶紧走！听说正在搜捕我们，为的砸了尤汉纳家那桩事。啊，他在这儿！起来，我的父亲，跟我走。今天的杀伐，比起明天日头底下要见的，不算什么。你可知道，一大批基督徒——从山里逃出来的——进了城求庇护。好些德鲁兹人，山里的、豪兰的，都追到了这儿。方才穿街过巷，我就撞见他们一伙。都是能征惯战的壮士，兵器齐整像正规兵。他们同我们一样，恨那些偶像教徒。……起来，穆斯塔法，走！人家知道我们常来这地方，这么大的节期前夜叫人拿了去，臊也臊死。……起来，我说！你怎么了？病了？说话！这是怎么了，努尔？」
那女人攀住他的胳膊。
「仁慈的安拉！我怕他是快不行了。他刚回来时像鬼附了体，又喊又嚎，双手乱舞。我好容易才按住他。这会儿他像昏死过去一样；看不见我，听不见我，比婴孩还软。」
「我保他只是乏了。安拉若肯，我自有法子叫醒他。他如同我父亲，这地方对他凶险。」
他大步走到穆斯塔法盘腿坐着的地方——老头正咕哝着句子的碎片，眼睛发直地瞪着那碗肉饭。他攋住老人的肩，俯下身，提起嗓门，像要追上那游走的魂灵，把它喊回来。
「你害不害臊，我的父亲！」他喊，「你今天亲手杀了仇敌，为教门打得那样英勇，如今倒像多愁多病的闺女似的病倒晕倒。安拉作证，我都替你臊得慌！醒醒，穆斯塔法！这地方不稳便。兵们——叫安拉劈了他们！——这会儿兴许正寻我们。待在这儿就得被捉、下狱，明天的荣耀一场全误了。伊斯兰的义愤烧得像一场大火，要烧尽不信教者。

===Original===

From the hour of sunrise the slaying will begin. Men will look for thee, O my father, in the front of the battle. They will marvel greatly and say one to another, ‘Where now is that old lion which devoured Yuhanna, the pig?’ They will look for thee to lead them on; it were a sin to disappoint them. Up, O Mustafa! The danger grows with every minute. Awake!﻿—y’Allah!﻿—for the faith of Muhammed!”
The last words were of magic virtue. The dying embers of the old man’s wit leapt up at them in lurid flame. With a cry he sprang to his feet, staring wild-eyed at Saïd.
“Dìn Muhammed!﻿—I slew him! O glorious left hand! Allah is bountiful! Yes, I hear thee, my son, and I understand. I was asleep, not so? I was weary and so I fell asleep, and methought the angel of death was with me. But it was a dream surely. I will go with thee, O my eyes, whither thou wilt, so that there be men to kill﻿—fat men like him, who lie on beds of down﻿—Ha, ha!﻿—while I who slew him am used to lie on the hard-trodden ground. I must be strong, sayest thou? Now, by my beard, that is a foolish word; for who is stronger than Mustafa? ’Hanna was weaker for I slew him easily, witness Allah and the bloodstains on my left hand. O glorious hand! But it is true what thou sayest, that a man’s strength must be nourished with meat. Of course, I will eat; and tomorrow I will do great slaughter﻿—thou and I together, O my soul. O blessed left hand! Allah is bountiful!”
He swallowed the food hastily by great mouthfuls, with no signs of relish. When the bowl was empty Nûr brought him a cup of hot coffee, which he gulped down in like manner. He grew reasonable, taking counsel with Saïd as to the best place for them to lie till morning. The old woman, seeing him fairly in the way of health, wished them both a happy night, and returned to the upper chamber to look after the girl Ferideh, whose moans and lamentations, though unheeded in the greater anxiety attending the beggar’s plight, had long been audible.
“Take care that she do herself no mischief: she is a very tigress!” Saïd called after her as he and his adopted father stepped out into the night. They went stealthily, by narrow ways the moonbeams seldom fathomed, to a small tavern kept by a Muslim, which was towards the Christian quarter. Others of the insurgents had likewise chosen that place for their night’s shelter. There were blithe greetings.

===Chinese===

「日头一出，开杀。人人要在阵前找你，我的父亲。他们要诧异，要彼此打听：『吞了尤汉纳那猪猡的老狮子呢？』他们指望你领头上；叫他们失望是罪过。起来，穆斯塔法！危险一刻大似一刻。醒醒！——呀，安拉！——为了穆罕默德的教门！」
末了几个字有符咒的效验。老人心智里将熄的余烬，扑地蹿起一蓬幽幽的火。他喊了一声，跳起来，狂眼瞪着赛义德。
「穆罕默德的教门！——我杀了他！光荣的左手！安拉大能！是，我听见了，我的儿，我明白。我方才是睡着了，不是么？乏了，就睡着了，梦里还当死神来到了我跟前。那准是梦。我跟你去，我的眼珠儿，你要去哪儿就去哪儿，只要有得人杀——杀肥人，像他那样睡鸭绒垫的——哈，哈！——杀了他的我，睡惯了夯实的泥地。你说我得养足力气？喍，凭我的胡子，这是傻话；谁比穆斯塔法更有力？汉纳就弱得多——我轻轻松松杀了他，安拉和我左手上的血渍作证。光荣的手啊！不过你有一句也对：人的力气得拿肉喂。当然，我吃；明天我大杀一场——你和我一起，我的魂。有福的左手！安拉大能！」
他大口大口急急地把饭吞下去，没有一点滋味的样子。碗空了，努尔又端来一杯热咖啡，他也照样灌了下去。他讲道理了，同赛义德商量着哪儿最稳妥，可以捱到天亮。老太婆见他算是回了阳，祝了两人一夜平安，回阁楼照看姑娘菲丽黛去了——姑娘的呜咽哀哭早听得见了，只是先前众人都悬着叫花子的安危，没人顾上。
「当心她别伤了自个儿：她是头地道的母虎！」赛义德朝她背影喊，一边同义父跨进夜里。两人蹑着脚，专走月光量不到底的窄路，到一家穆斯林开的小酒馆去，馆子朝着基督徒区那边。起事的人里，也有别的相中了这地方过夜。一片欢快的招呼。

===Original===

A discussion was going on, in which Mustafa, having no care to rest, joined eagerly. But Saïd, being very drowsy, yawned cavernously at all that was said. He soon stretched his length on the floor and fell fast asleep.

===Chinese===

一场议论正开着，穆斯塔法毫无睡意，热切地加入进去。赛义德却困得不行，人家说什么他都洞开着嘴打哈欠。不多时，他把身子往地上一横，睡得死沉。

### XXV / 二十五
===Original===

“Dìn! Dìn! Dìn Muhammed!”﻿ ﻿… “Allah! Allah!”﻿ ﻿… “Death to the unbelievers!”﻿ ﻿… “Perish the Nazarenes!”
Saïd awoke to the consciousness of a frightful uproar streaming in with a sunbeam through the open door. The whole city was filled with it﻿—wrapt in it as in a mist. Frenzied shouts for Allah and the Prophet, devilish yells and cries of exultation mingled with the run of a great multitude in the street without, the distant beat of a drum and a sound of desultory firing.
The tavern, in deep shadow, was empty save for the old beggar, who stood over him brandishing a curved knife like a sickle in his sound hand, while with the withered he pointed to the piece of an iron bar which lay on the ground close to Saïd. A fierce devil looked out at his eyes.
“Arise, O sluggard!” he cried with a mad laugh. “Is this a time to sleep and be lazy? Come, let us out! There will be blood!﻿—blood﻿—blood of unbelievers to flush the streets like water! Aha, the dogs of the city shall drink rare wine tonight!”
